					<div id="mw-content-text" class="mw-body-content mw-content-ltr" lang="en" dir="ltr"><div class="mw-parser-output"><div class="shortdescription nomobile noexcerpt noprint searchaux" style="display:none">Caribbean island constituent country of the Netherlands</div>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">This article is about the island country. For the former colony/territory comprising Curaçao and five other islands, see <a href="/wiki/Cura%C3%A7ao_and_Dependencies" title="Curaçao and Dependencies">Curaçao and Dependencies</a>. For the liqueur, see <a href="/wiki/Cura%C3%A7ao_(liqueur)" title="Curaçao (liqueur)">Curaçao (liqueur)</a>. For the Los Angeles based retail store chain, see <a href="/wiki/Curacao_(retail_store)" title="Curacao (retail store)">Curacao (retail store)</a>.</div>

side</a></th><td class="infobox-data">right</td></tr><tr class="mergedtoprow"><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Telephone_numbers_in_Cura%C3%A7ao" class="mw-redirect" <p><b>Curaçao</b> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;k&#39; in &#39;kind&#39;">k</span></span>(<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/j/: &#39;y&#39; in &#39;yes&#39;">j</span></span>)<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ʊər/: &#39;our&#39; in &#39;tour&#39;">ʊər</span><span title="/ə/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;about&#39;">ə</span><span title="/ˌ/: secondary stress follows">ˌ</span><span title="&#39;s&#39; in &#39;sigh&#39;">s</span><span title="/oʊ/: &#39;o&#39; in &#39;code&#39;">oʊ</span></span>,<span class="wrap"> </span>-<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˌ/: secondary stress follows">ˌ</span><span title="&#39;s&#39; in &#39;sigh&#39;">s</span><span title="/aʊ/: &#39;ou&#39; in &#39;mouth&#39;">aʊ</span></span>,<span class="wrap"> </span><span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˌ/: secondary stress follows">ˌ</span><span title="&#39;k&#39; in &#39;kind&#39;">k</span></span>(<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/j/: &#39;y&#39; in &#39;yes&#39;">j</span></span>)<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ʊər/: &#39;our&#39; in &#39;tour&#39;">ʊər</span><span title="/ə/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;about&#39;">ə</span><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;s&#39; in &#39;sigh&#39;">s</span><span title="/oʊ/: &#39;o&#39; in &#39;code&#39;">oʊ</span></span>,<span class="wrap"> </span>-<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;s&#39; in &#39;sigh&#39;">s</span><span title="/aʊ/: &#39;ou&#39; in &#39;mouth&#39;">aʊ</span></span>/</a></span></span> <a href="/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key" title="Help:Pronunciation respelling key"><i title="English pronunciation respelling"><span style="font-size:90%">KURE</span>-ə-soh, -&#8288;sow, -&#8288;<span style="font-size:90%">SOH</span>, -&#8288;<span style="font-size:90%">SOW</span></i></a>;<sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> <small>Dutch:&#160;</small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="nl-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Dutch" title="Help:IPA/Dutch">[kyːraːˈsʌu, kuːr-]</a></span>&#32;<span class="nowrap" style="font-size:85%">(<span class="unicode haudio"><span class="fn"><span style="white-space:nowrap;margin-right:.25em;"><a href="/wiki/File:Nl-Cura%C3%A7ao.ogg" title="About this sound"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/11px-Loudspeaker.svg.png" decoding="async" width="11" height="11" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/17px-Loudspeaker.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/22px-Loudspeaker.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="20" data-file-height="20" /></a></span><a href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/0/01/Nl-Cura%C3%A7ao.ogg" class="internal" title="Nl-Curaçao.ogg">listen</a></span></span>)</span>;<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Papiamento_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Papiamento language">Papiamento</a>: <i lang="pap">Kòrsou</i>, <small></small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="pap-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA" title="Help:IPA">[ˈkɔrsɔu̯]</a></span>), officially the <b>Country of Curaçao</b> (<a href="/wiki/Dutch_language" title="Dutch language">Dutch</a>: <i lang="nl">Land Curaçao</i>;<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Papiamento_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Papiamento language">Papiamento</a>: <i lang="pap">Pais Kòrsou</i>),<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> is a <a href="/wiki/Lesser_Antilles" title="Lesser Antilles">Lesser Antilles</a> <a href="/wiki/Island_country" title="Island country">island country</a> in the southern <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Sea" title="Caribbean Sea">Caribbean Sea</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Caribbean" title="Dutch Caribbean">Dutch Caribbean</a> region, about 65&#160;km (40&#160;mi) north of the <a href="/wiki/Venezuela" title="Venezuela">Venezuela</a> coast. It is a constituent country of the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands" title="Kingdom of the Netherlands">Kingdom of the Netherlands</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> Together with <a href="/wiki/Aruba" title="Aruba">Aruba</a> and <a href="/wiki/Bonaire" title="Bonaire">Bonaire</a>, it forms the <a href="/wiki/ABC_islands_(Leeward_Antilles)" title="ABC islands (Leeward Antilles)">ABC islands</a>. Collectively, Curaçao, Aruba, and other Dutch islands in the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean" title="Caribbean">Caribbean</a> are often called the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Caribbean" title="Dutch Caribbean">Dutch Caribbean</a>. It is the largest of the ABC islands in both area and population as well as the largest of the Dutch Caribbean.<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Curaçao was formerly part of the <a href="/wiki/Cura%C3%A7ao_and_Dependencies" title="Curaçao and Dependencies">Curaçao and Dependencies</a> <a href="/wiki/Colony" title="Colony">colony</a> from 1815 to 1954 and later the <a href="/wiki/Netherlands_Antilles" title="Netherlands Antilles">Netherlands Antilles</a> from 1954 to 2010, as Island Territory of Curaçao (Dutch: <i lang="nl">Eilandgebied Curaçao</i>, Papiamento: <i lang="pap">Teritorio Insular di Kòrsou</i>),<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> and is now formally called the Country of Curaçao.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao_12-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> It includes the main island of Curaçao and the much smaller, uninhabited island of <a href="/wiki/Klein_Cura%C3%A7ao" title="Klein Curaçao">Klein Curaçao</a> ("Little Curaçao").<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao_12-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> Curaçao has a population of 158,665 (January 2019 est.),<sup id="cite_ref-rijksoverheid.nl_3-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rijksoverheid.nl-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> with an area of 444&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (171&#160;sq&#160;mi); its capital is <a href="/wiki/Willemstad" title="Willemstad">Willemstad</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao_12-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Etymology">Etymology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Etymology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>One explanation for the island's name is that <i>Curaçao</i> was the <a href="/wiki/Autonym_(onomastics)" class="mw-redirect" title="Autonym (onomastics)">autonym</a> by which its indigenous peoples identified themselves.<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> Early <a href="/wiki/Spanish_colonization_of_the_Americas" title="Spanish colonization of the Americas">Spanish</a> accounts support this theory, referring to the indigenous peoples as <i>Indios Curaçaos</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-curacao-nature_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-curacao-nature-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>From 1525, the island was featured on Spanish maps as <i>Curaçote, Curasaote, Curasaore</i>, and even <i>Curacaute</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> By the 17th century, it appeared on most maps as <i>Curaçao</i> or <i>Curazao.</i><sup id="cite_ref-curacao-nature_17-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-curacao-nature-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> On a map created by <a href="/wiki/Hieronymus_Cock" title="Hieronymus Cock">Hieronymus Cock</a> in 1562 in <a href="/wiki/Antwerp" title="Antwerp">Antwerp</a>, the island was called <i>Qúracao</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A persistent but undocumented story claims the following: in the 16th and 17th centuries—the early years of European exploration—when <a href="/wiki/Sailor" title="Sailor">sailors</a> on long voyages got <a href="/wiki/Scurvy" title="Scurvy">scurvy</a> from lack of <a href="/wiki/Vitamin_C" title="Vitamin C">vitamin C</a>, sick Portuguese or Spanish sailors were left on the island now known as Curaçao. When their ship returned, some had recovered, probably after eating vitamin C-rich fruit there. From then on, the Portuguese allegedly referred to the island as <i>Ilha da Curação</i> (Island of Healing)<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao_12-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> or the Spanish as <i>Isla de la Curación</i>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (February 2023)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: History">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/History_of_Cura%C3%A7ao" title="History of Curaçao">History of Curaçao</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cura%C3%A7ao_and_Dependencies" title="Curaçao and Dependencies">Curaçao and Dependencies</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Caribbean" title="Dutch Caribbean">Dutch Caribbean</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Pre-colonial">Pre-colonial</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Pre-colonial">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:1562_Americae-Gutierrez_map_10hrs-inn_Sth-Florida-Cuba-Spagnola-Benezuela-to-Lesser-Antilles.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1f/1562_Americae-Gutierrez_map_10hrs-inn_Sth-Florida-Cuba-Spagnola-Benezuela-to-Lesser-Antilles.jpg/220px-1562_Americae-Gutierrez_map_10hrs-inn_Sth-Florida-Cuba-Spagnola-Benezuela-to-Lesser-Antilles.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="193" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1f/1562_Americae-Gutierrez_map_10hrs-inn_Sth-Florida-Cuba-Spagnola-Benezuela-to-Lesser-Antilles.jpg/330px-1562_Americae-Gutierrez_map_10hrs-inn_Sth-Florida-Cuba-Spagnola-Benezuela-to-Lesser-Antilles.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1f/1562_Americae-Gutierrez_map_10hrs-inn_Sth-Florida-Cuba-Spagnola-Benezuela-to-Lesser-Antilles.jpg/440px-1562_Americae-Gutierrez_map_10hrs-inn_Sth-Florida-Cuba-Spagnola-Benezuela-to-Lesser-Antilles.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2660" data-file-height="2330" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:1562_Americae-Gutierrez_map_10hrs-inn_Sth-Florida-Cuba-Spagnola-Benezuela-to-Lesser-Antilles.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Map from 1562 with Curaçao indicated as Qúracao</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Cura%C3%A7ao1836.png" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cf/Cura%C3%A7ao1836.png/220px-Cura%C3%A7ao1836.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="183" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cf/Cura%C3%A7ao1836.png/330px-Cura%C3%A7ao1836.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cf/Cura%C3%A7ao1836.png/440px-Cura%C3%A7ao1836.png 2x" data-file-width="2366" data-file-height="1964" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Cura%C3%A7ao1836.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Map of Curaçao in 1836</div></div></div>
<p>The original inhabitants of Curaçao were the <a href="/wiki/Arawak" title="Arawak">Arawak</a> and <a href="/wiki/Caquetio" title="Caquetio">Caquetio</a> <a href="/wiki/Amerindians" class="mw-redirect" title="Amerindians">Amerindians</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-The_History_of_Curaçao_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_History_of_Curaçao-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> Their ancestors had migrated to the island from the mainland of <a href="/wiki/South_America" title="South America">South America</a>, probably hundreds of years before Europeans first arrived.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Spanish_colonization">Spanish colonization</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Spanish colonization">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The first Europeans recorded as seeing the island were members of a Spanish expedition under the leadership of <a href="/wiki/Alonso_de_Ojeda" title="Alonso de Ojeda">Alonso de Ojeda</a> in 1499.<sup id="cite_ref-The_History_of_Curaçao_20-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_History_of_Curaçao-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> The Spaniards <a href="/wiki/Enslaving" class="mw-redirect" title="Enslaving">enslaved</a> most of the Caquetios(Arawak) for <a href="/wiki/Forced_labour" title="Forced labour">forced labour</a> in their <a href="/wiki/Hispaniola" title="Hispaniola">Hispaniola</a> colony, but paid little attention to the island itself.<sup id="cite_ref-The_History_of_Curaçao_20-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_History_of_Curaçao-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> In 1515, almost all of the 2,000 Caquetios living there were also transported to Hispaniola as slaves.
</p><p>Starting in 1499, Curaçao served as a bridge for the Spanish exploration and conquest of territories in northern South America. The Spanish settled on the island in 1527. Throughout the 16th century, they ruled Curaçao as an insular part of the province of Venezuela, governing it from the mainland before gradually abandoning it as colonization of the continent progressed. One of the oldest written references to the island is in the archives of the main public city registry of <a href="/wiki/Caracas" title="Caracas">Caracas</a>, Venezuela. A document dated 9 December 1595 states that Francisco Montesinos, <a href="/wiki/Priest" title="Priest">priest</a> and <a href="/wiki/Vicar" title="Vicar">vicar</a> of "the Yslas de Curasao, <a href="/wiki/Aruba" title="Aruba">Aruba</a> and <a href="/wiki/Bonaire" title="Bonaire">Bonaire</a>" conferred his <a href="/wiki/Power_of_attorney" title="Power of attorney">power of attorney</a> to Pedro Gutiérrez de Lugo, a Caracas resident, to collect his ecclesiastic salary from the Royal Treasury of <a href="/wiki/Philip_II_of_Spain" title="Philip II of Spain">King Philip II of Spain</a>.
</p><p>The Spanish introduced numerous tree, plant and animal species to Curaçao, including horses, sheep, goats, pigs and cattle from Europe and other Spanish colonies. In general, imported sheep, goats and cattle did relatively well. Cattle were herded by Caquetios and Spaniards and roamed freely in the kunuku plantations and <a href="/wiki/Savanna" title="Savanna">savannas</a>.
</p><p>Not all imported species fared equally well, and the Spanish also learned to use Caquetio crops and agricultural methods, as well as those from other Caribbean islands. Though historical sources point to thousands of people living on the island, agricultural yields were disappointing; this and the lack of precious metals in the salt mines led the Spanish to call Curaçao "the useless island".
</p><p>Over time, the number of Spaniards living on Curaçao decreased while the number of aboriginal inhabitants stabilized. Presumably through natural growth, return and colonization, the Caquetio population then began to increase. In the last decades of Spanish occupation, Curaçao was used as a large cattle ranch. At that point, Spaniards lived around <a href="/wiki/Santa_Barbara,_Cura%C3%A7ao" class="mw-redirect" title="Santa Barbara, Curaçao">Santa Barbara</a>, Santa Ana and in the villages in the western part of the island, while the Caquetios are thought to have lived scattered all over the island.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Dutch_colonial_rule">Dutch colonial rule</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Dutch colonial rule">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 1634, during the <a href="/wiki/Eighty_Years%27_War" title="Eighty Years&#39; War">Eighty Years' War</a> of independence between the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Republic" title="Dutch Republic">Republic of the Netherlands</a> and Spain, the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_West_India_Company" title="Dutch West India Company">Dutch West India Company</a> under Admiral <a href="/wiki/Johann_van_Walbeeck" class="mw-redirect" title="Johann van Walbeeck">Johann van Walbeeck</a> invaded the island; the Spanish surrendered in San Juan in August. Approximately 30 Spaniards and many indigenous people were then deported to Santa Ana de Coro in Venezuela. About 30 Taíno families were allowed to live on the island while Dutch colonists started settling there.<sup id="cite_ref-The_History_of_Curaçao_20-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_History_of_Curaçao-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p>The Dutch West India Company founded the capital of Willemstad on the banks of an inlet called the Schottegat; the <a href="/wiki/Natural_harbour" class="mw-redirect" title="Natural harbour">natural harbour</a> proved an ideal place for trade. Commerce and shipping—and <a href="/wiki/Piracy_in_the_Caribbean" title="Piracy in the Caribbean">piracy</a>—became Curaçao's most important economic activities. Later, <a href="/wiki/Salt_mines" class="mw-redirect" title="Salt mines">salt mining</a> became a major industry, the mineral being a lucrative export at the time.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> From 1662, the Dutch West India Company made Curaçao a centre of the <a href="/wiki/Atlantic_slave_trade" title="Atlantic slave trade">Atlantic slave trade</a>, often bringing slaves from West Africa to the island, before selling them elsewhere in the Caribbean and <a href="/wiki/Spanish_Main" title="Spanish Main">Spanish Main</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-The_History_of_Curaçao_20-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_History_of_Curaçao-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Sephardic_Jews" title="Sephardic Jews">Sephardic Jews</a> fleeing persecution in <a href="/wiki/Expulsion_of_the_Jews_from_Spain" class="mw-redirect" title="Expulsion of the Jews from Spain">Spain</a> and <a href="/wiki/Expulsion_of_the_Jews_from_Portugal" class="mw-redirect" title="Expulsion of the Jews from Portugal">Portugal</a> sought safe haven in <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Brazil" title="Dutch Brazil">Dutch Brazil</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Republic" title="Dutch Republic">Dutch Republic</a>. Many settled in Curaçao, where they made significant contributions to its civil society, cultural development and economic prosperity.<sup id="cite_ref-history_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-history-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the <a href="/wiki/Franco-Dutch_War" title="Franco-Dutch War">Franco-Dutch War</a> of 1672–78, French Count <a href="/wiki/Jean_II_d%27Estr%C3%A9es" title="Jean II d&#39;Estrées">Jean II d'Estrées</a> planned to attack Curaçao. His fleet—12 <a href="/wiki/Man-of-war" title="Man-of-war">men-of-war</a>, three <a href="/wiki/Fire_ship" title="Fire ship">fire ships</a>, two transports, a <a href="/wiki/Hospital_ship" title="Hospital ship">hospital ship</a>, and 12 <a href="/wiki/Privateer" title="Privateer">privateers</a>—met with disaster, losing seven men-of-war and two other ships when they struck <a href="/wiki/Reef" title="Reef">reefs</a> off the <a href="/wiki/Las_Aves_archipelago" class="mw-redirect" title="Las Aves archipelago">Las Aves archipelago</a>. The serious navigational error occurred on 11 May 1678, a week after the fleet set sail from <a href="/wiki/Saint_Kitts" title="Saint Kitts">Saint Kitts</a>. To commemorate its narrow escape from invasion, Curaçao marked the events with a day of thanksgiving, which was celebrated for decades into the 18th century.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Cura%C3%A7ao,_Broad_Street.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8e/Cura%C3%A7ao%2C_Broad_Street.jpg/220px-Cura%C3%A7ao%2C_Broad_Street.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="161" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8e/Cura%C3%A7ao%2C_Broad_Street.jpg/330px-Cura%C3%A7ao%2C_Broad_Street.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8e/Cura%C3%A7ao%2C_Broad_Street.jpg/440px-Cura%C3%A7ao%2C_Broad_Street.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1745" data-file-height="1279" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Cura%C3%A7ao,_Broad_Street.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Willemstad, 1885</div></div></div>
<p>Many Dutch colonists grew affluent from the slave trade, building impressive colonial buildings in the capital of Willemstad; the city is now a <a href="/wiki/UNESCO_World_Heritage_Site" class="mw-redirect" title="UNESCO World Heritage Site">UNESCO World Heritage Site</a>.
</p><p>In 1795, <a href="/wiki/Cura%C3%A7ao_Slave_Revolt_of_1795" title="Curaçao Slave Revolt of 1795">a major slave revolt</a> took place under the leaders <a href="/wiki/Tula_(Cura%C3%A7ao)" title="Tula (Curaçao)">Tula Rigaud</a>, Louis Mercier, Bastian Karpata, and Pedro Wakao. Up to 4,000 slaves in northwest Curaçao revolted, with more than 1,000 taking part in extended gunfights. After a month, the slave owners were able to suppress the revolt.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Luis_Bri%C3%B3n_2012_000.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/be/Luis_Bri%C3%B3n_2012_000.jpg/220px-Luis_Bri%C3%B3n_2012_000.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="240" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/be/Luis_Bri%C3%B3n_2012_000.jpg/330px-Luis_Bri%C3%B3n_2012_000.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/be/Luis_Bri%C3%B3n_2012_000.jpg/440px-Luis_Bri%C3%B3n_2012_000.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2272" data-file-height="2480" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Luis_Bri%C3%B3n_2012_000.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Luis_Bri%C3%B3n" title="Luis Brión">Luis Brión</a>, a Curaçao-born Venezuelan admiral</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:The_colorful_buildings_of_the_Handelskade_in_Willemstad,_Cura%C3%A7ao.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/The_colorful_buildings_of_the_Handelskade_in_Willemstad%2C_Cura%C3%A7ao.jpg/220px-The_colorful_buildings_of_the_Handelskade_in_Willemstad%2C_Cura%C3%A7ao.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="114" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/The_colorful_buildings_of_the_Handelskade_in_Willemstad%2C_Cura%C3%A7ao.jpg/330px-The_colorful_buildings_of_the_Handelskade_in_Willemstad%2C_Cura%C3%A7ao.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/The_colorful_buildings_of_the_Handelskade_in_Willemstad%2C_Cura%C3%A7ao.jpg/440px-The_colorful_buildings_of_the_Handelskade_in_Willemstad%2C_Cura%C3%A7ao.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2539" data-file-height="1317" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:The_colorful_buildings_of_the_Handelskade_in_Willemstad,_Cura%C3%A7ao.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The colorful buildings of the Handelskade in <a href="/wiki/Willemstad" title="Willemstad">Willemstad</a>, Curaçao</div></div></div>
<p>Curaçao's proximity to South America resulted in interaction with cultures of the coastal areas more than a century after the independence of the Netherlands from Spain. Architectural similarities can be seen between 19th century Willemstad neighborhoods and the nearby Venezuelan city of <a href="/wiki/Santa_Ana_de_Coro" class="mw-redirect" title="Santa Ana de Coro">Coro</a> in <a href="/wiki/Falc%C3%B3n_State" class="mw-redirect" title="Falcón State">Falcón State</a>, which has also been designated a World Heritage Site. Netherlands established economic ties with the <a href="/wiki/Viceroyalty_of_New_Granada" title="Viceroyalty of New Granada">Viceroyalty of New Granada</a> that included the present-day countries of <a href="/wiki/Colombia" title="Colombia">Colombia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Venezuela" title="Venezuela">Venezuela</a>. In the 19th century, Curaçaoans such as <a href="/wiki/Manuel_Piar" title="Manuel Piar">Manuel Piar</a> and <a href="/wiki/Luis_Bri%C3%B3n" title="Luis Brión">Luis Brión</a> were prominently engaged in the wars of independence of both Venezuela and Colombia. Political refugees from the mainland, such as <a href="/wiki/Simon_Bolivar" class="mw-redirect" title="Simon Bolivar">Simon Bolivar</a>, regrouped in Curaçao.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During the 18th and 19th centuries, the British attacked the island several times, most notably in <a href="/wiki/Invasion_of_Cura%C3%A7ao_(1800)" title="Invasion of Curaçao (1800)">1800</a>, 1804, and from 1807 to 1815.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> Stable Dutch rule returned in 1815 at the end of the <a href="/wiki/Napoleonic_wars" class="mw-redirect" title="Napoleonic wars">Napoleonic wars</a>, when the island was incorporated into the colony of <a href="/wiki/Cura%C3%A7ao_and_Dependencies" title="Curaçao and Dependencies">Curaçao and Dependencies</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-curacao-travelguide.com_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-curacao-travelguide.com-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Dutch <a href="/wiki/Timeline_of_abolition_of_slavery_and_serfdom" title="Timeline of abolition of slavery and serfdom">abolished slavery</a> in 1863, causing vast changes in the economy with the shift to <a href="/wiki/Wage_labour" title="Wage labour">wage labour</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-curacao-travelguide.com_26-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-curacao-travelguide.com-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> Some Curaçao inhabitants emigrated to other islands, such as <a href="/wiki/Cuba" title="Cuba">Cuba</a>, to work in <a href="/wiki/Sugar_plantations_in_the_Caribbean" title="Sugar plantations in the Caribbean">sugarcane plantations</a>. Other former slaves had nowhere to go and continued working for plantation owners under the <a href="/wiki/Tenant_farmer" title="Tenant farmer">tenant farmer</a> system,<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> in which former slaves leased land from former masters, paying most of their <a href="/wiki/Harvest" title="Harvest">harvest</a> to owners as rent. The system lasted until the early 20th century.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Historically, Dutch was not widely spoken on the island outside of the colonial administration, but its use increased in the late 19th and early 20th centuries.<sup id="cite_ref-Putte_28-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Putte-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> Students on Curaçao, Aruba, and Bonaire were taught predominantly in Spanish until the late 17th century, when the British took all three islands. Teaching of Spanish was restored when Dutch rule resumed in 1815. Also, efforts were made to introduce widespread bilingual Dutch and <a href="/wiki/Papiamento" title="Papiamento">Papiamentu</a> education in the late 19th century.<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="20th_and_21st_centuries">20th and 21st centuries</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: 20th and 21st centuries">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>When oil was discovered in the Venezuelan <a href="/wiki/Maracaibo_Basin" title="Maracaibo Basin">Maracaibo Basin</a> town of Mene Grande in 1914, Curaçao's economy was dramatically altered. In the early years, both <a href="/wiki/Royal_Dutch_Shell" class="mw-redirect" title="Royal Dutch Shell">Shell</a> and <a href="/wiki/Exxon" class="mw-redirect" title="Exxon">Exxon</a> held drilling <a href="/wiki/Concession_(contract)" title="Concession (contract)">concessions</a> in Venezuela, which ensured a constant supply of crude oil to refineries in Aruba and Curaçao. Crude oil production in Venezuela was inexpensive. Both Shell and Exxon were <a href="/wiki/Vertical_integration" title="Vertical integration">vertically integrated</a> and controlled the entire industry, from pumping, transporting, and refining to sales. The refineries on Aruba and Curaçao operated in global markets and were profitable partly because of the margin between the production costs of crude oil and the revenues made off of products. This provided a safety net for losses incurred through inefficiency or excessive operating costs at the refineries.<sup id="cite_ref-The_History_of_Curaçao_20-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_History_of_Curaçao-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Reliable_sources" title="Wikipedia:Reliable sources"><span title="The material near this tag may rely on an unreliable source. (February 2022)">unreliable source?</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>In 1929, Curaçao was attacked by <a href="/wiki/Venezuelans" title="Venezuelans">Venezuelan</a> rebel commander <a href="/wiki/Rafael_Sim%C3%B3n_Urbina" title="Rafael Simón Urbina">Rafael Simón Urbina</a>, who, with 250 soldiers, captured the fort. The Venezuelans plundered weapons, ammunition, and the island's treasury. They also managed to capture the Governor of the island, Leonardus Albertus Fruytier (1882-1972), and hauled him off to Venezuela on a stolen American ship, <i>Maracaibo</i>. Fruytier was criticized and had to resign as governor. After returning to the Netherlands, he settled for a position as chief inspector in <a href="/wiki/Maastricht" title="Maastricht">Maastricht</a>. The Dutch increased their military presence on the island.<sup id="cite_ref-mindef_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mindef-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1954, Curaçao and other Dutch Caribbean colonies were joined together to form the <a href="/wiki/Netherlands_Antilles" title="Netherlands Antilles">Netherlands Antilles</a>. Discontent with Curaçao's seemingly subordinate relationship to the Netherlands, ongoing <a href="/wiki/Racial_discrimination" title="Racial discrimination">racial discrimination</a>, and a rise in <a href="/wiki/Unemployment" title="Unemployment">unemployment</a> owing to layoffs in the oil industry led to a <a href="/wiki/1969_Cura%C3%A7ao_uprising" title="1969 Curaçao uprising">series of riots</a> in 1969.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> The riots resulted in two deaths, numerous injuries and severe damage in Willemstad. In response, the Dutch government introduced far-reaching reforms, allowing Afro-Curaçaoans greater influence over the island's political and economic life, and increased the prominence of the local Papiamentu language.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Dutch_soldier,_1969_Cura%C3%A7ao_uprising.png" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ad/Dutch_soldier%2C_1969_Cura%C3%A7ao_uprising.png/220px-Dutch_soldier%2C_1969_Cura%C3%A7ao_uprising.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="180" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ad/Dutch_soldier%2C_1969_Cura%C3%A7ao_uprising.png/330px-Dutch_soldier%2C_1969_Cura%C3%A7ao_uprising.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/a/ad/Dutch_soldier%2C_1969_Cura%C3%A7ao_uprising.png 2x" data-file-width="352" data-file-height="288" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Dutch_soldier,_1969_Cura%C3%A7ao_uprising.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A Dutch soldier on patrol in Willemstad following the 1969 riots</div></div></div>
<p>Curaçao experienced an economic downturn in the early 1980s. Shell's refinery on the island operated with significant losses from 1975 to 1979, and again from 1982 to 1985. Persistent losses, global <a href="/wiki/Overproduction" title="Overproduction">overproduction</a>, stronger competition, and low market expectations threatened the refinery's future. In 1985, after 70 years, Royal Dutch Shell decided to end its activities on Curaçao. This came at a crucial moment. Curaçao's fragile economy had been stagnant for some time. Several revenue-generating sectors suffered even more during this period: tourism from Venezuela collapsed after the <a href="/wiki/Devaluation" title="Devaluation">devaluation</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Venezuelan_bol%C3%ADvar" title="Venezuelan bolívar">bolivar</a>, and a slowdown in the transportation sector had deleterious effects on the Antillean Airline Company and the Curaçao Dry Dock Company. The offshore financial services industry also experienced a downturn due to new U.S. tax laws.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>In the mid-1980s, Shell sold its refinery for the symbolic amount of one <a href="/wiki/Netherlands_Antillean_guilder" title="Netherlands Antillean guilder">Antillean guilder</a> to a local government <a href="/wiki/Consortium" title="Consortium">consortium</a>. In recent years, the aging refinery has been the subject of lawsuits alleging that its emissions, including <a href="/wiki/Sulfur_dioxide" title="Sulfur dioxide">sulfur dioxide</a> and <a href="/wiki/Atmospheric_particulate_matter" class="mw-redirect" title="Atmospheric particulate matter">particulate matter</a>, far exceed safety standards.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_34-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> The government consortium leases the refinery to the Venezuelan <a href="/wiki/PDVSA" title="PDVSA">PDVSA</a> state oil company.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_34-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Continuing economic hardship in the late 1990s and early 2000s resulted in much emigration to the Netherlands.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 1 July 2007, Curaçao was due to become a country within the Kingdom of the Netherlands, like Aruba and the Netherlands Antilles. On 28 November 2006, the change was delayed when the island council rejected a clarification memorandum on the process. A new island council ratified this agreement on 9 July 2007.<sup id="cite_ref-approved_36-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-approved-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> On 15 December 2008, Curaçao was again scheduled to become a separate country within the Kingdom of the Netherlands. A non-binding referendum on the move was held in Curaçao on 15 May 2009; 52% of voters supported it.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Since_the_dissolution_of_the_Netherlands_Antilles">Since the dissolution of the Netherlands Antilles</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Since the dissolution of the Netherlands Antilles">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Dissolution_of_the_Netherlands_Antilles" title="Dissolution of the Netherlands Antilles">dissolution of the Netherlands Antilles</a> came into effect on 10 October 2010.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> Curaçao became a country within the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands" title="Kingdom of the Netherlands">Kingdom of the Netherlands</a>, with the kingdom retaining responsibility for <a href="/wiki/Defense_(military)" class="mw-redirect" title="Defense (military)">defence</a> and <a href="/wiki/Foreign_policy" title="Foreign policy">foreign policy</a>. The kingdom was also tasked with overseeing the island's finances under a <a href="/wiki/Debt_relief" title="Debt relief">debt-relief</a> arrangement agreed upon between the two.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> Curaçao's first <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Cura%C3%A7ao" title="Prime Minister of Curaçao">prime minister</a> was <a href="/wiki/Gerrit_Schotte" title="Gerrit Schotte">Gerrit Schotte</a>. He was succeeded in 2012 by <a href="/wiki/Stanley_Betrian" title="Stanley Betrian">Stanley Betrian</a>, <i>ad interim</i>. After the 2012 elections, <a href="/wiki/Daniel_Hodge" title="Daniel Hodge">Daniel Hodge</a> became the third prime minister on 31 December 2012.<sup id="cite_ref-Hodge_41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Hodge-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> He led a <a href="/wiki/Demissionary_cabinet" title="Demissionary cabinet">demissionary cabinet</a> until 7 June 2013, when a new cabinet under the leadership of <a href="/wiki/Ivar_Asjes" title="Ivar Asjes">Ivar Asjes</a> was sworn in.<sup id="cite_ref-Asjes_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Asjes-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Although Curaçao is autonomous, the Netherlands has intervened in its affairs to ensure that parliamentary elections are held and to assist in finalizing accurate budgets. In July 2017, Curaçaoan Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Eugene_Rhuggenaath" title="Eugene Rhuggenaath">Eugene Rhuggenaath</a> said he wanted Curaçao to take full responsibility over its affairs, but asked for more cooperation and assistance from the Netherlands, with suggestions for more innovative approaches to help Curaçao succeed and increase its <a href="/wiki/Standard_of_living" title="Standard of living">standard of living</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> The Dutch government reminded the Curaçaoan government that it had provided assistance with oil refinery negotiations with the Chinese "on numerous occasions".<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The 2020 <a href="/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Cura%C3%A7ao" title="COVID-19 pandemic in Curaçao">COVID-19 pandemic</a> resulted in <a href="/wiki/Austerity_measures" class="mw-redirect" title="Austerity measures">austerity measures</a>. Curaçao had to impose spending cuts to qualify for additional aid from the Netherlands.<sup id="cite_ref-ntr_46-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ntr-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> As part of the austerity package, the Government of Curaçao announced a 12.5% cut in benefits for <a href="/wiki/Civil_servants" class="mw-redirect" title="Civil servants">civil servants</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-head_47-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-head-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> On 24 June 2020, a group of civil servants, together with <a href="/wiki/Waste_collector" title="Waste collector">waste collectors</a> from Selikor, marched to <a href="/wiki/Fort_Amsterdam_(Cura%C3%A7ao)" title="Fort Amsterdam (Curaçao)">Fort Amsterdam</a> and demanded to speak with Rhuggenaath.<sup id="cite_ref-head_47-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-head-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> The demonstration turned into a <a href="/wiki/Riot" title="Riot">riot</a>, and police cleared the square in front of Fort Amsterdam<sup id="cite_ref-nu_48-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-nu-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> with <a href="/wiki/Tear_gas" title="Tear gas">tear gas</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> The city centre of <a href="/wiki/Willemstad" title="Willemstad">Willemstad</a> was later <a href="/wiki/Looting" title="Looting">looted</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-nu_48-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-nu-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> 48 people were arrested,<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> the city districts of <a href="/wiki/Punda" class="mw-redirect" title="Punda">Punda</a> and <a href="/wiki/Otrobanda" title="Otrobanda">Otrobanda</a> were placed under <a href="/wiki/Lockdown" title="Lockdown">lockdown</a> for the night, and a general <a href="/wiki/Curfew" title="Curfew">curfew</a> was declared from 20:30 to 06:00.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Geography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/List_of_populated_places_in_Cura%C3%A7ao" title="List of populated places in Curaçao">List of populated places in Curaçao</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Curacao_Dushi_(34800928041).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/33/Curacao_Dushi_%2834800928041%29.jpg/220px-Curacao_Dushi_%2834800928041%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="124" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/33/Curacao_Dushi_%2834800928041%29.jpg/330px-Curacao_Dushi_%2834800928041%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/33/Curacao_Dushi_%2834800928041%29.jpg/440px-Curacao_Dushi_%2834800928041%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3733" data-file-height="2097" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Curacao_Dushi_(34800928041).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Aerial view of the coast of Curaçao</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Cura%C3%A7ao2021OSM.png" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e0/Cura%C3%A7ao2021OSM.png/220px-Cura%C3%A7ao2021OSM.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="183" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e0/Cura%C3%A7ao2021OSM.png/330px-Cura%C3%A7ao2021OSM.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e0/Cura%C3%A7ao2021OSM.png/440px-Cura%C3%A7ao2021OSM.png 2x" data-file-width="6666" data-file-height="5555" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Cura%C3%A7ao2021OSM.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Enlargeable, detailed map of Curaçao</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Curacao_Iguana_iguana_2021_2.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8b/Curacao_Iguana_iguana_2021_2.jpg/220px-Curacao_Iguana_iguana_2021_2.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8b/Curacao_Iguana_iguana_2021_2.jpg/330px-Curacao_Iguana_iguana_2021_2.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8b/Curacao_Iguana_iguana_2021_2.jpg/440px-Curacao_Iguana_iguana_2021_2.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5896" data-file-height="3931" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Curacao_Iguana_iguana_2021_2.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Green Iguana from Curaçao</div></div></div>
<p>Curaçao, as well as the rest of the <a href="/wiki/ABC_islands_(Leeward_Antilles)" title="ABC islands (Leeward Antilles)">ABC islands</a> and <a href="/wiki/Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="Trinidad and Tobago">Trinidad and Tobago</a>, lies on the <a href="/wiki/Continental_shelf" title="Continental shelf">continental shelf</a> of South America. It is a thin island with a generally hilly topography; the highest point is <a href="/wiki/Christoffelberg" title="Christoffelberg">Christoffelberg</a> in the northwest, with a peak at 372&#160;m (1,220&#160;ft) above <a href="/wiki/Sea_level" title="Sea level">sea level</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> The coastline's bays, inlets and hot springs offer a source of natural minerals, thermal conditions, and seawater used in <a href="/wiki/Hydrotherapy" title="Hydrotherapy">hydrotherapy</a> and <a href="/wiki/Mesotherapy" title="Mesotherapy">mesotherapy</a>, making the island one of many <a href="/wiki/Balneotherapy" title="Balneotherapy">balneoclimateric</a> areas in the region. Off the southeast coast lies the small, flat island of <a href="/wiki/Klein_Cura%C3%A7ao" title="Klein Curaçao">Klein Curaçao</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao_12-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Flora">Flora</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Flora">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Curaçao's flora differ from typical tropical island vegetation. <a href="/wiki/Guajira-Barranquilla_xeric_scrub" class="mw-redirect" title="Guajira-Barranquilla xeric scrub">Guajira-Barranquilla xeric scrub</a> is the most notable, with various forms of <a href="/wiki/Cacti" class="mw-redirect" title="Cacti">cacti</a>, thorny shrubs, <a href="/wiki/Evergreen" title="Evergreen">evergreen</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Watapana" class="mw-redirect" title="Watapana">watapana</a> trees (<i>Libidibia coriaria</i>; called <i>divi-divi</i> on Aruba), which are characteristic of the ABC islands and the national symbol of Aruba. <i><a href="/wiki/Brassavola_nodosa" title="Brassavola nodosa">Brassavola nodosa</a></i> is a <a href="/wiki/Drought_tolerance" title="Drought tolerance">drought-tolerant</a> species of <i><a href="/wiki/Brassavola" title="Brassavola">Brassavola</a>,</i> one of the few <a href="/wiki/Orchid" title="Orchid">orchids</a> present in the ABC islands. Cacti include <i><a href="/wiki/Melocactus" title="Melocactus">Melocactus</a></i> and <i><a href="/wiki/Opuntia" title="Opuntia">Opuntia</a></i> species such as <i><a href="/wiki/Opuntia_stricta" title="Opuntia stricta">Opuntia stricta</a></i>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:302px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Melocactus_macracanthos_subsp._macracanthos_(6253748197).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d4/Melocactus_macracanthos_subsp._macracanthos_%286253748197%29.jpg/300px-Melocactus_macracanthos_subsp._macracanthos_%286253748197%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="300" height="200" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d4/Melocactus_macracanthos_subsp._macracanthos_%286253748197%29.jpg/450px-Melocactus_macracanthos_subsp._macracanthos_%286253748197%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d4/Melocactus_macracanthos_subsp._macracanthos_%286253748197%29.jpg/600px-Melocactus_macracanthos_subsp._macracanthos_%286253748197%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3888" data-file-height="2592" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Melocactus_macracanthos_subsp._macracanthos_(6253748197).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Melocactus macracanthos in Curaçao</div></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Fauna">Fauna</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Fauna">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Rodents_of_the_Caribbean#Curaçao" class="mw-redirect" title="Rodents of the Caribbean">Rodents of the Caribbean §&#160;Curaçao</a></div>
<p>Curaçao is <a href="/wiki/Semi-arid_climate" title="Semi-arid climate">semi-arid</a>, and as such has not supported the numerous tropical species of mammals, birds, and lizards most associated with <a href="/wiki/Rainforest" title="Rainforest">rainforests</a>. Dozens of species of <a href="/wiki/Hummingbird" title="Hummingbird">hummingbirds</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bananaquit" title="Bananaquit">bananaquits</a>, <a href="/wiki/Oriole_blackbird" title="Oriole blackbird">orioles</a>, and the larger <a href="/wiki/Tern" title="Tern">terns</a>, <a href="/wiki/Heron" title="Heron">herons</a>, <a href="/wiki/Egret" title="Egret">egrets</a>, and even <a href="/wiki/Flamingo" title="Flamingo">flamingos</a> make their homes near ponds or in coastal areas. The trupial, a black bird with a bright orange underbelly and white swatches on its wings, is common to Curaçao. The <a href="/wiki/Mockingbird" title="Mockingbird">mockingbird</a>, called <i>chuchubi</i> in Papiamentu, resembles the North American mockingbird, with a long white-grey tail and a grey back. Near shorelines, big-billed <a href="/wiki/Brown_pelican" title="Brown pelican">brown pelicans</a> feed on fish. Other seabirds include several types of <a href="/wiki/Gull" title="Gull">gulls</a> and large <a href="/wiki/Cormorant" title="Cormorant">cormorants</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Other than field mice, small rabbits, and cave bats, Curaçao's most notable animal is the <a href="/wiki/White-tailed_deer" title="White-tailed deer">white-tailed deer</a>. This deer is related to the American white-tailed deer, or Virginia deer, found in areas from North America through Central America and the Caribbean, and as far south as Bolivia. It can be a large deer, some reaching six feet (2&#160;m) in length and three feet (0.9&#160;m) in height, and weighing as much as 300 pounds (140&#160;kg). It has a long tail with a white underside, and is the only type of deer on the island. It has been a <a href="/wiki/Protected_species" class="mw-redirect" title="Protected species">protected species</a> since 1926, and an estimated 200 live on Curaçao. They are found in many parts of the island, but most notably at the west end's Christoffel Park, where about 70% of the herd resides. <a href="/wiki/Archaeology" title="Archaeology">Archaeologists</a> believe the deer were brought from South America to Curaçao by its original inhabitants, the Arawaks.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>There are several species of <a href="/wiki/Iguana" title="Iguana">iguana</a>, light green in colour with shimmering shades of aqua along the belly and sides, found lounging in the sun across the island. The iguanas found on Curaçao serve not only as a scenic attraction but, unlike many islands that gave up the practice years ago, remain hunted for food. Along the west end of the island's north shore are several inlets that have become home to breeding <a href="/wiki/Sea_turtle" title="Sea turtle">sea turtles</a>. These turtles are protected by the park system in Shete Boka Park, and can be visited accompanied by park rangers.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate">Climate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Climate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Curaçao has a <a href="/wiki/Semi-arid_climate" title="Semi-arid climate">hot, semi-arid climate</a> (<a href="/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification" title="Köppen climate classification">Köppen climate classification</a> <i>BSh</i>) with a <a href="/wiki/Dry_season" title="Dry season">dry season</a> from January to September and a <a href="/wiki/Wet_season" title="Wet season">wet season</a> from October to December.<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> Rainfall is scarce, only 450 millimeters (12 inches) per year; in particular, the rainy season is drier than it normally is in tropical climates; during the dry season, it almost never rains. Owing to the scarcity of rainfall, the landscape of Curaçao is arid; especially on the north coast of the island. Temperatures are relatively constant, with small differences measured throughout the year. The <a href="/wiki/Trade_winds" title="Trade winds">trade winds</a> cool the island during the day and warm it at night. The coolest month is January with an average temperature of 26.6&#160;°C or 80&#160;°F; the hottest is September with an average temperature of 29.1&#160;°C or 84&#160;°F. The year's average maximum temperature is 31.4&#160;°C or 89&#160;°F. The year's average temperature is 25.7&#160;°C or 78&#160;°F. The seawater around Curaçao averages around 27&#160;°C (81&#160;°F) and is coolest (avg. 25.9&#160;°C [78.6&#160;°F]) from February to March, and hottest (avg. 28.2&#160;°C [82.8&#160;°F]) from September to October.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Because Curaçao lies North of the <a href="/wiki/Intertropical_Convergence_Zone" title="Intertropical Convergence Zone">Intertropical Convergence Zone</a> and in an area of low-level divergence where winds flow parallel to the coast, its climate is much drier than expected for the northeastern side of a continent at its latitude. Rainfall is also extremely variable from year to year,<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> being strongly linked to the <a href="/wiki/El_Ni%C3%B1o_Southern_Oscillation" class="mw-redirect" title="El Niño Southern Oscillation">El Niño Southern Oscillation</a>. As little as 200 millimetres or 8 inches may fall in a strong El Niño year, but as much as 1,150 millimetres or 45 inches is not unknown in powerful <a href="/wiki/La_Ni%C3%B1a" title="La Niña">La Niña</a> years.
</p><p>Curaçao lies outside the Atlantic's <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Alley" title="Hurricane Alley">Hurricane Alley</a> but is still occasionally affected by <a href="/wiki/Hurricanes" class="mw-redirect" title="Hurricanes">hurricanes</a>, as with Hurricane <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Hazel" title="Hurricane Hazel">Hazel</a> in 1954, <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Anna_(1961)" title="Hurricane Anna (1961)">Anna</a> in 1961, <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Felix_(2007)" class="mw-redirect" title="Hurricane Felix (2007)">Felix</a> in 2007, and <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Omar_(2008)" class="mw-redirect" title="Hurricane Omar (2008)">Omar</a> in 2008. No hurricane has made <a href="/wiki/Landfall" title="Landfall">landfall</a> in Curaçao since the US <a href="/wiki/National_Hurricane_Center" title="National Hurricane Center">National Hurricane Center</a> started tracking hurricanes. Curaçao has, however, been directly affected by pre-hurricane tropical storms several times; the latest being Hurricane <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Tomas" title="Hurricane Tomas">Tomas</a> in 2010, <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Cesar-Douglas" class="mw-redirect" title="Hurricane Cesar-Douglas">Cesar</a> in 1996, <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Joan%E2%80%93Miriam" title="Hurricane Joan–Miriam">Joan</a> in 1988, <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Cora" title="Hurricane Cora">Cora</a> and <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Greta%E2%80%93Olivia" title="Hurricane Greta–Olivia">Greta</a> in 1978, <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Edith_(1971)" title="Hurricane Edith (1971)">Edith</a> and <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Irene_(1971)" class="mw-redirect" title="Hurricane Irene (1971)">Irene</a> in 1971, and <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Francelia" title="Hurricane Francelia">Francelia</a> in 1969. Tomas brushed past Curaçao as a tropical storm, dropping as much as 265&#160;mm (10.4&#160;in) of rain on the island, nearly half its annual precipitation in a single day.<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> This made Tomas one of the wettest events in the island's history,<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> as well as one of the most devastating; its flooding killed two people and caused over NAƒ50&#160;million (US$28&#160;million) in damage.<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to the Emissions Database for Global Atmospheric Research, average <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_carbon_dioxide_emissions_per_capita" title="List of countries by carbon dioxide emissions per capita">carbon dioxide emissions per person</a> on the island were 52 tonnes in 2018, the second highest in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Meteo, the Curaçao weather department, provides up-to-date information about weather conditions via its <a href="/wiki/Website" title="Website">website</a> and <a href="/wiki/Mobile_app" title="Mobile app">mobile apps</a> for <a href="/wiki/IOS" title="IOS">iOS</a> and <a href="/wiki/Android_(operating_system)" title="Android (operating system)">Android</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
