<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate_change">Climate change</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Climate change">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Average temperatures have risen sharply in the past 40 years in the Caribbean Netherlands and Curaçao has experienced more warm days and fewer cooler nights.<sup id="cite_ref-:2_61-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Intergovernmental_Panel_on_Climate_Change" title="Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change">Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change</a> predicts that should air temperatures increase by 1.4 degrees, there will be a 5% to 6% decrease in rainfall, increased frequency and intensity of extreme weather events (including a 66% increase in hurricane intensity), and a 0.5- to 0.6-meter sea-level rise in the Caribbean Netherlands.<sup id="cite_ref-:2_61-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
</div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Geology">Geology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Geology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Geology_of_Cura%C3%A7ao" title="Geology of Curaçao">Geology of Curaçao</a></div>
<p>The northern <a href="/wiki/Seabed" title="Seabed">seabed</a> drops steeply within 60&#160;m (200&#160;ft) of the Curaçaoan shore. This drop-off is known as the "blue edge".
</p><p>On Curaçao, four major <a href="/wiki/Geological_formation" title="Geological formation">geological formations</a> can be found: the lava formation, the Knip formation, the Mid-Curaçao formation and limestone formations.<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Curaçao lies within the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_large_igneous_province" title="Caribbean large igneous province">Caribbean large igneous province</a> (CLIP) with key exposures of those lavas existing on the island consisting of the Curaçao Lava Formation (CLF). The CLF consists of 5&#160;km of <a href="/wiki/Pillow_lava" title="Pillow lava">pillow lavas</a> with some <a href="/wiki/Basalt" title="Basalt">basalt</a> <a href="/wiki/Intrusion" class="mw-redirect" title="Intrusion">intrusions</a>. The ages of these rocks include 89 <a href="/wiki/Mega-annum" class="mw-redirect" title="Mega-annum">Ma</a> for the lavas and 75 Ma for the <a href="/wiki/Poikilitic" class="mw-redirect" title="Poikilitic">poikilitic</a> <a href="/wiki/Sill_(geology)" title="Sill (geology)">sills</a>, though some sequences may have erupted as late as 62–66 Ma, placing them in the <a href="/wiki/Cretaceous" title="Cretaceous">Cretaceous</a> period. Their composition includes <a href="/wiki/Picrite" class="mw-redirect" title="Picrite">picrite</a> pillows at the base, followed by <a href="/wiki/Tholeiitic" class="mw-redirect" title="Tholeiitic">tholeiitic</a> lavas, then <a href="/wiki/Hyaloclastite" title="Hyaloclastite">hyaloclastites</a>, then the poikilitic sills. The CLF was gradually <a href="/wiki/Geologic_uplift" class="mw-redirect" title="Geologic uplift">uplifted</a> until <a href="/wiki/Eocene" title="Eocene">Eocene</a>-<a href="/wiki/Miocene" title="Miocene">Miocene</a> limestone caps formed, before final exposure above sea level. <a href="/wiki/Christoffelberg" title="Christoffelberg">Christoffelberg</a> and the Zevenbergen (Seven Hills) portion of the island have exposures of the Knip <a href="/wiki/Formation_(geology)" class="mw-redirect" title="Formation (geology)">Formation</a>. This formation includes deepwater deposits of <a href="/wiki/Calcareous" title="Calcareous">calcareous</a> <a href="/wiki/Sand" title="Sand">sands</a> and fine <a href="/wiki/Clay" title="Clay">clays</a>, capped by <a href="/wiki/Siliceous" class="mw-redirect" title="Siliceous">siliceous</a> <a href="/wiki/Chert" title="Chert">chert</a> containing <a href="/wiki/Radiolarian" class="mw-redirect" title="Radiolarian">radiolarians</a>. Middle Curaçao contains <a href="/wiki/Alluvial" class="mw-redirect" title="Alluvial">alluvial</a> soils from eroded CLF and limestone.<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Beaches">Beaches</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Beaches">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:302px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Grote_Knip_Kenepa_Grandi_Curacao_(36561697451).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/73/Grote_Knip_Kenepa_Grandi_Curacao_%2836561697451%29.jpg/300px-Grote_Knip_Kenepa_Grandi_Curacao_%2836561697451%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="300" height="200" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/73/Grote_Knip_Kenepa_Grandi_Curacao_%2836561697451%29.jpg/450px-Grote_Knip_Kenepa_Grandi_Curacao_%2836561697451%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/73/Grote_Knip_Kenepa_Grandi_Curacao_%2836561697451%29.jpg/600px-Grote_Knip_Kenepa_Grandi_Curacao_%2836561697451%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5355" data-file-height="3570" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Grote_Knip_Kenepa_Grandi_Curacao_(36561697451).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Grote Knip Beach</div></div></div>
<p>Curaçao has a large number of beaches. Most are on the south side of the island. The best known are:
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r998391716">.mw-parser-output .div-col{margin-top:0.3em;column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .div-col-small{font-size:90%}.mw-parser-output .div-col-rules{column-rule:1px solid #aaa}.mw-parser-output .div-col dl,.mw-parser-output .div-col ol,.mw-parser-output .div-col ul{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .div-col li,.mw-parser-output .div-col dd{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}</style><div class="div-col" style="column-width: 16em;">
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Baya_Beach" title="Baya Beach">Baya Beach</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Blue_Bay,_Cura%C3%A7ao" title="Blue Bay, Curaçao">Blue Bay</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Boca_Sami" class="mw-redirect" title="Boca Sami">Boca Sami</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Daaibooi" title="Daaibooi">Daaibooi</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Grote_Knip" title="Grote Knip">Grote Knip</a> (Kenepa Grandi)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Kleine_Knip" title="Kleine Knip">Kleine Knip</a> (Kenepa Chiki)</li>
<li>Kokomo Beach</li>
<li>Mambo Beach</li>
<li>Piscaderabaai</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Playa_Forti" title="Playa Forti">Playa Forti</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Playa_Jeremi" title="Playa Jeremi">Playa Jeremi</a></li>
<li>Playa Kas Abao</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Playa_Kalki" title="Playa Kalki">Playa Kalki</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Playa_Kanoa" title="Playa Kanoa">Playa Kanoa</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Playa_Lagun" title="Playa Lagun">Playa Lagun</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Playa_Porto_Marie" title="Playa Porto Marie">Playa Porto Marie</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Playa_Santa_Cruz" title="Playa Santa Cruz">Playa Santa Cruz</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Santa_Barbara_Beach,_Cura%C3%A7ao" title="Santa Barbara Beach, Curaçao">Playa Santa Barbara</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Seaquarium_Beach" title="Seaquarium Beach">Seaquarium Beach</a></li>
<li>Sint Michielsbaai</li>
<li>Vaersenbaai</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Westpunt" title="Westpunt">Westpunt</a></li></ul>
</div>
<p>Curaçao has a total of 37 beaches.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Architecture">Architecture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Architecture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The island has diverse architectural styles reflecting the influence of the various historical rulers over the region, including Spain, the Netherlands, with more modern elements under Western influence primarily including the United States and other European countries. This ranges from <a href="/wiki/Ruins" title="Ruins">ruins</a> and colonial buildings to modern infrastructure.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Forts">Forts</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Forts">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Curacao_Fort_Beekenburg_Luftbild_(34873603326).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5c/Curacao_Fort_Beekenburg_Luftbild_%2834873603326%29.jpg/220px-Curacao_Fort_Beekenburg_Luftbild_%2834873603326%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5c/Curacao_Fort_Beekenburg_Luftbild_%2834873603326%29.jpg/330px-Curacao_Fort_Beekenburg_Luftbild_%2834873603326%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5c/Curacao_Fort_Beekenburg_Luftbild_%2834873603326%29.jpg/440px-Curacao_Fort_Beekenburg_Luftbild_%2834873603326%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3033" data-file-height="2273" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Curacao_Fort_Beekenburg_Luftbild_(34873603326).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Fort Beekenburg, Caracas Bay, Curaçao</div></div></div>
<p>When the Dutch arrived in 1634, they built forts at key points around the island to protect themselves from foreign powers, privateers, and pirates. Six of the best-preserved forts can still be seen today:
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Fort_Amsterdam_(Cura%C3%A7ao)" title="Fort Amsterdam (Curaçao)">Fort Amsterdam</a> (1635)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Fort_Beekenburg" class="mw-redirect" title="Fort Beekenburg">Fort Beekenburg</a> (1703)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Fort_Nassau_(Cura%C3%A7ao)" title="Fort Nassau (Curaçao)">Fort Nassau</a> (1797)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Waterfort" title="Waterfort">Waterfort</a> (1826)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Otrobanda#Rif_Fort" title="Otrobanda">Rif Fort</a> (1828)<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Fort_Piscadera" class="mw-redirect" title="Fort Piscadera">Fort Piscadera</a> (built between 1701 and 1704)</li></ul>
<p>In 1957, the hotel <a href="/wiki/Van_der_Valk_Plaza_Cura%C3%A7ao" class="mw-redirect" title="Van der Valk Plaza Curaçao">Van der Valk Plaza Curaçao</a> was built on top of the Waterfort.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Rif Fort is located opposite of the Waterfort, across the <a href="/wiki/Otrobanda" title="Otrobanda">Otrobanda</a> harbour entrance. It contains restaurants and shops, and in 2009, the Renaissance Curaçao Resort and Casino opened next to it.<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Government">Government</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Government">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Cura%C3%A7ao" title="Government of Curaçao">Government of Curaçao</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Edificio_del_Parlamento_de_Cura%C3%A7ao,_ubicado_en_el_distrito_de_Punda,_Willemstad.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/Edificio_del_Parlamento_de_Cura%C3%A7ao%2C_ubicado_en_el_distrito_de_Punda%2C_Willemstad.jpg/220px-Edificio_del_Parlamento_de_Cura%C3%A7ao%2C_ubicado_en_el_distrito_de_Punda%2C_Willemstad.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="293" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/Edificio_del_Parlamento_de_Cura%C3%A7ao%2C_ubicado_en_el_distrito_de_Punda%2C_Willemstad.jpg/330px-Edificio_del_Parlamento_de_Cura%C3%A7ao%2C_ubicado_en_el_distrito_de_Punda%2C_Willemstad.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/ff/Edificio_del_Parlamento_de_Cura%C3%A7ao%2C_ubicado_en_el_distrito_de_Punda%2C_Willemstad.jpg/440px-Edificio_del_Parlamento_de_Cura%C3%A7ao%2C_ubicado_en_el_distrito_de_Punda%2C_Willemstad.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2736" data-file-height="3648" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Edificio_del_Parlamento_de_Cura%C3%A7ao,_ubicado_en_el_distrito_de_Punda,_Willemstad.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Curaçao Parliament Building (<i>Parlamento di Kòrsou</i>) located in the Punda district, Willemstad</div></div></div>
<p>Curaçao is a <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands#Constituent_countries" title="Kingdom of the Netherlands">constituent country</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands" title="Kingdom of the Netherlands">Kingdom of the Netherlands</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao_12-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> Its governance takes place in a framework of a <a href="/wiki/Parliamentary_system" title="Parliamentary system">parliamentary</a> <a href="/wiki/Representative_democracy" title="Representative democracy">representative democracy</a>. The <a href="/wiki/King_of_the_Netherlands" title="King of the Netherlands">King of the Netherlands</a> is the <a href="/wiki/Head_of_state" title="Head of state">head of state</a>, represented locally by a <a href="/wiki/Governor_of_Cura%C3%A7ao" title="Governor of Curaçao">governor</a>, with the <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Cura%C3%A7ao" title="Prime Minister of Curaçao">Prime Minister of Curaçao</a> serving as <a href="/wiki/Head_of_government" title="Head of government">head of government</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao_12-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Executive_power" class="mw-redirect" title="Executive power">Executive power</a> is exercised by the government. <a href="/wiki/Legislative_power" class="mw-redirect" title="Legislative power">Legislative power</a> is vested in both the government and <a href="/wiki/Estates_of_Cura%C3%A7ao" class="mw-redirect" title="Estates of Curaçao">parliament</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Judiciary" title="Judiciary">judiciary</a> is <a href="/wiki/Judicial_independence" title="Judicial independence">independent</a> of the executive and the legislature. Convicted <a href="/wiki/Felony" title="Felony">felons</a> are held at the <a href="/wiki/Sentro_di_Detenshon_i_Korekshon_K%C3%B2rsou" class="mw-redirect" title="Sentro di Detenshon i Korekshon Kòrsou">Curaçao Centre for Detention and Correction</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (October 2013)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Curaçao has full autonomy over most matters; the exceptions are outlined in the <a href="/wiki/Charter_for_the_Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands" title="Charter for the Kingdom of the Netherlands">Charter for the Kingdom of the Netherlands</a> under the title "<a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands#Kingdom_affairs" title="Kingdom of the Netherlands">Kingdom affairs</a>".<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (October 2013)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Military">Military</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Military">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Luchtfoto_van_de_marinebasis_Parera_op_Cura%C3%A7ao.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1e/Luchtfoto_van_de_marinebasis_Parera_op_Cura%C3%A7ao.jpg/220px-Luchtfoto_van_de_marinebasis_Parera_op_Cura%C3%A7ao.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1e/Luchtfoto_van_de_marinebasis_Parera_op_Cura%C3%A7ao.jpg/330px-Luchtfoto_van_de_marinebasis_Parera_op_Cura%C3%A7ao.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1e/Luchtfoto_van_de_marinebasis_Parera_op_Cura%C3%A7ao.jpg/440px-Luchtfoto_van_de_marinebasis_Parera_op_Cura%C3%A7ao.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2784" data-file-height="1848" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Luchtfoto_van_de_marinebasis_Parera_op_Cura%C3%A7ao.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Parera Naval Base</div></div></div>
<p>Defence of the island is the responsibility of the Netherlands.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao_12-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Netherlands_Armed_Forces" title="Netherlands Armed Forces">Netherlands Armed Forces</a> deploy both ground and naval units in the Caribbean with some of these forces based on Curacao. These forces include: 
</p>
<ul><li>a company of the <a href="/wiki/Royal_Netherlands_Army" title="Royal Netherlands Army">Royal Netherlands Army</a> on Curaçao on a rotational basis;</li>
<li>a Fast Raiding Interception and Special Forces Craft (FRISC) troop (fast boats);</li>
<li>a guardship, normally a <a href="/wiki/Holland-class_offshore_patrol_vessel" title="Holland-class offshore patrol vessel">Holland-class offshore patrol vessel</a>, from the <a href="/wiki/Royal_Netherlands_Navy" title="Royal Netherlands Navy">Royal Netherlands Navy</a> on station in the Caribbean on a rotational basis;</li>
<li>the Royal Netherlands Navy support vessel <a href="/wiki/HNLMS_Pelikaan_(A804)" title="HNLMS Pelikaan (A804)">HNLMS Pelikaan</a>;</li>
<li>Curmil (Curaçaoan) militia elements;</li>
<li>Elements of a <a href="/wiki/Royal_Marechaussee" title="Royal Marechaussee">Royal Marechaussee</a> brigade of the Armed Forces.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<p>Two <a href="/wiki/Royal_Netherlands_Navy" title="Royal Netherlands Navy">Dutch</a> naval bases, Parera and <a href="/wiki/Marinekazerne_Suffisant" title="Marinekazerne Suffisant">Suffisant</a>, are located on the island of Curaçao. Officers of the <a href="/wiki/Military_of_Aruba" title="Military of Aruba">Arubaanse Militie</a> complete additional training on Curaçao.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>On the west side of <a href="/wiki/Cura%C3%A7ao_International_Airport" title="Curaçao International Airport">Curaçao International Airport</a> are hangars for the two <a href="/wiki/De_Havilland_Canada_Dash_8" title="De Havilland Canada Dash 8">Bombardier Dash 8</a> Maritime Patrol Aircraft and two <a href="/wiki/AgustaWestland_AW139" title="AgustaWestland AW139">AgustaWestland AW139</a> helicopters of the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Caribbean_Coast_Guard" title="Dutch Caribbean Coast Guard">Dutch Caribbean Coast Guard</a>. Until 2007, the site was a <a href="/wiki/Royal_Netherlands_Navy" title="Royal Netherlands Navy">Royal Netherlands Navy</a> <a href="/wiki/Air_base" title="Air base">air base</a> which operated for 55 years with a wide variety of aircraft, including <a href="/wiki/Fairey_Firefly" title="Fairey Firefly">Fireflies</a>, <a href="/wiki/Grumman_TBF_Avenger" title="Grumman TBF Avenger">Avengers</a>, <a href="/wiki/Grumman_S-2_Tracker" title="Grumman S-2 Tracker">Trackers</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lockheed_P-2_Neptune" title="Lockheed P-2 Neptune">Neptunes</a>, <a href="/wiki/Fokker_F27_Friendship" title="Fokker F27 Friendship">Fokker F-27s</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lockheed_P-3_Orion" title="Lockheed P-3 Orion">P-3C Orions</a>, <a href="/wiki/Fokker_50" title="Fokker 50">Fokker F-60s</a> and several helicopter types. After the political decision to sell off all Orions, the air base wasn't needed anymore.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The west end of the airport is a <a href="/wiki/USAF" class="mw-redirect" title="USAF">USAF</a> <a href="/wiki/Forward_Operating_Site" class="mw-redirect" title="Forward Operating Site">Forward Operating Location</a> (FOL).<sup id="cite_ref-auto_71-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> The base hosts <a href="/wiki/Airborne_early_warning_and_control" title="Airborne early warning and control">Airborne Warning And Control System</a> (AWACS), <a href="/wiki/Cargo_aircraft" title="Cargo aircraft">cargo aircraft</a>, <a href="/wiki/Aerial_refueling" title="Aerial refueling">aerial refueling</a> planes, and <a href="/wiki/Reconnaissance_aircraft" title="Reconnaissance aircraft">reconnaissance aircraft</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_71-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> Until 1999, the USAF operated a small fleet of <a href="/wiki/General_Dynamics_F-16_Fighting_Falcon" title="General Dynamics F-16 Fighting Falcon">F-16</a> fighters from the FOL.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> The <a href="/wiki/Pacific_Architects_and_Engineers" title="Pacific Architects and Engineers">PAE</a> corporation runs base operations at the FOL.<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Conscription">Conscription</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Conscription">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Suffisant Naval Base has facilities used for <a href="/wiki/Conscription" title="Conscription">conscription</a> in the Caribbean. There has been no military conscription since 1997, but a form of <a href="/wiki/Civil_conscription" title="Civil conscription">civil conscription</a> has replaced it, compelling underprivileged young Antilleans to undertake professional training.<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Politics">Politics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Politics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>After being part of the <a href="/wiki/Netherlands_Antilles" title="Netherlands Antilles">Netherlands Antilles</a>, Curaçao became autonomous, along with <a href="/wiki/Sint_Maarten" title="Sint Maarten">Sint Maarten</a> island, while the less-populated islands of <a href="/wiki/Bonaire" title="Bonaire">Bonaire</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sint_Eustatius" title="Sint Eustatius">Sint Eustatius</a> and <a href="/wiki/Saba_(island)" title="Saba (island)">Saba</a> remained <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Netherlands" title="Caribbean Netherlands">special municipalities</a> governed by the Netherlands.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_74-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-74">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Economy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Cura%C3%A7ao" title="Economy of Curaçao">Economy of Curaçao</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Bohrinsel_Curacao_(34800864151).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f6/Bohrinsel_Curacao_%2834800864151%29.jpg/220px-Bohrinsel_Curacao_%2834800864151%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="124" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f6/Bohrinsel_Curacao_%2834800864151%29.jpg/330px-Bohrinsel_Curacao_%2834800864151%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f6/Bohrinsel_Curacao_%2834800864151%29.jpg/440px-Bohrinsel_Curacao_%2834800864151%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3992" data-file-height="2242" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Bohrinsel_Curacao_(34800864151).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Offshore oil platform in Curaçao</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Historic_Area_of_Willemstad,_Inner_City_and_Harbour,_Cura%C3%A7ao-139159.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c8/Historic_Area_of_Willemstad%2C_Inner_City_and_Harbour%2C_Cura%C3%A7ao-139159.jpg/220px-Historic_Area_of_Willemstad%2C_Inner_City_and_Harbour%2C_Cura%C3%A7ao-139159.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c8/Historic_Area_of_Willemstad%2C_Inner_City_and_Harbour%2C_Cura%C3%A7ao-139159.jpg/330px-Historic_Area_of_Willemstad%2C_Inner_City_and_Harbour%2C_Cura%C3%A7ao-139159.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c8/Historic_Area_of_Willemstad%2C_Inner_City_and_Harbour%2C_Cura%C3%A7ao-139159.jpg/440px-Historic_Area_of_Willemstad%2C_Inner_City_and_Harbour%2C_Cura%C3%A7ao-139159.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1600" data-file-height="1200" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Historic_Area_of_Willemstad,_Inner_City_and_Harbour,_Cura%C3%A7ao-139159.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Historic area of Willemstad, declared a <a href="/wiki/World_Heritage_Site" title="World Heritage Site">World Heritage Site</a> by UNESCO in 1997</div></div></div>
<p>Curaçao has an <a href="/wiki/Open_economy" title="Open economy">open economy</a>; its most important sectors are <a href="/wiki/Tourism" title="Tourism">tourism</a>, <a href="/wiki/International_trade" title="International trade">international trade</a>, <a href="/wiki/Freight_transport" title="Freight transport">shipping services</a>, <a href="/wiki/Oil_refinery" title="Oil refinery">oil refining</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Oil_terminal" title="Oil terminal">oil storage</a> and <a href="/wiki/Bunkering" title="Bunkering">bunkering</a>, and international <a href="/wiki/Financial_services" title="Financial services">financial services</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao_12-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> Venezuelan state oil company <a href="/wiki/PDVSA" title="PDVSA">PDVSA</a>'s lease on the island's oil refinery expired in 2019; the facility employs 1,000 people, refining oil from <a href="/wiki/Venezuela" title="Venezuela">Venezuela</a> for export to the US and Asia.<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Schlumberger" title="Schlumberger">Schlumberger</a>, the world's largest oil field services company, is incorporated in Curaçao.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/w/index.php?title=Isla_oil_refinery&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Isla oil refinery (page does not exist)">Isla oil refinery</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raffinerie_de_Isla" class="extiw" title="fr:Raffinerie de Isla">fr</a>; <a href="https://nl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isla_(raffinaderij)" class="extiw" title="nl:Isla (raffinaderij)">nl</a>&#93;</span> is said to be responsible for Curaçao's position as one of the world's top five highest per capita CO<sub>2</sub> emission-producing countries.<sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Along with <a href="/wiki/Sint_Maarten" title="Sint Maarten">Sint Maarten</a>, Curaçao uses the <a href="/wiki/Netherlands_Antillean_guilder" title="Netherlands Antillean guilder">Netherlands Antillean guilder</a> as its currency.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao_12-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> Its economy is well-developed, supporting a high standard of living, ranking 46th in the world in terms of <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_(PPP)_per_capita" title="List of countries by GDP (PPP) per capita">GDP (PPP) per capita</a> and 27th in the world in terms of <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_(nominal)_per_capita" title="List of countries by GDP (nominal) per capita">nominal GDP per capita</a>. Curaçao possesses a <a href="/wiki/World_Bank_high-income_economy" title="World Bank high-income economy">high-income economy</a> as defined by the <a href="/wiki/World_Bank" title="World Bank">World Bank</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> 
Activities related to the port of Willemstad, such as the <a href="/wiki/Free_Trade_Zone" class="mw-redirect" title="Free Trade Zone">Free Trade Zone</a>, make significant contributions to the economy.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao_12-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> To achieve greater <a href="/wiki/Economic_diversity" title="Economic diversity">economic diversification</a>, the Curaçaoan government is increasing its efforts to attract more foreign investment.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao_12-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> This policy, called the "Open Arms" policy, features a heavy focus on attracting <a href="/wiki/Information_technology" title="Information technology">information technology</a> companies.<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Since 2016, reduced foreign demand for goods due to the ongoing <a href="/wiki/Venezuelan_protests_(2014%E2%80%93present)" class="mw-redirect" title="Venezuelan protests (2014–present)">unrest</a> and <a href="/wiki/Venezuelan_presidential_crisis" title="Venezuelan presidential crisis">political uncertainty</a> in Venezuela has led to decreased exports and increased domestic demand for goods and services, resulting in <a href="/wiki/Economic_stagnation" title="Economic stagnation">economic stagnation</a>. While many economic sectors contracted, expansion took place in the <a href="/wiki/Construction" title="Construction">construction</a>, <a href="/wiki/Financial_intermediary" title="Financial intermediary">financial intermediation</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Public_utility" title="Public utility">utilities</a> sectors.<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Tourism">Tourism</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Tourism">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:292px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Cura%C3%A7ao_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/Cura%C3%A7ao_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/290px-Cura%C3%A7ao_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="290" height="189" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/Cura%C3%A7ao_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/435px-Cura%C3%A7ao_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/Cura%C3%A7ao_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/580px-Cura%C3%A7ao_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Cura%C3%A7ao_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A proportional representation of the products exported from Curaçao in 2019</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Curacao_Jan_Thiel_(34526956140).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2f/Curacao_Jan_Thiel_%2834526956140%29.jpg/220px-Curacao_Jan_Thiel_%2834526956140%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="124" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2f/Curacao_Jan_Thiel_%2834526956140%29.jpg/330px-Curacao_Jan_Thiel_%2834526956140%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2f/Curacao_Jan_Thiel_%2834526956140%29.jpg/440px-Curacao_Jan_Thiel_%2834526956140%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3992" data-file-height="2242" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Curacao_Jan_Thiel_(34526956140).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Jan Thiel Beach</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Elkhorn_Coral_with_a_Yellowtail_Damselfish_in_the_Caribbean_Sea_in_Cura%C3%A7ao.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9c/Elkhorn_Coral_with_a_Yellowtail_Damselfish_in_the_Caribbean_Sea_in_Cura%C3%A7ao.jpg/220px-Elkhorn_Coral_with_a_Yellowtail_Damselfish_in_the_Caribbean_Sea_in_Cura%C3%A7ao.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="176" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9c/Elkhorn_Coral_with_a_Yellowtail_Damselfish_in_the_Caribbean_Sea_in_Cura%C3%A7ao.jpg/330px-Elkhorn_Coral_with_a_Yellowtail_Damselfish_in_the_Caribbean_Sea_in_Cura%C3%A7ao.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9c/Elkhorn_Coral_with_a_Yellowtail_Damselfish_in_the_Caribbean_Sea_in_Cura%C3%A7ao.jpg/440px-Elkhorn_Coral_with_a_Yellowtail_Damselfish_in_the_Caribbean_Sea_in_Cura%C3%A7ao.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2347" data-file-height="1878" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Elkhorn_Coral_with_a_Yellowtail_Damselfish_in_the_Caribbean_Sea_in_Cura%C3%A7ao.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Elkhorn_coral" title="Elkhorn coral">Elkhorn coral</a> with a Yellowtail Damselfish (<a href="/wiki/Microspathodon_chrysurus" title="Microspathodon chrysurus">Microspathodon chrysurus</a>) in the Caribbean Sea in Curaçao</div></div></div>
<p>While tourism plays a major role in Curaçao's economy, the island is less reliant on tourism than many other Caribbean countries. Most tourists come to Curaçao from the Netherlands, the eastern United States, South America and other Caribbean islands.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Curaçao was a Caribbean leader in <a href="/wiki/Cruise_ship" title="Cruise ship">cruise ship</a> tourism growth, with 610,186 cruise passengers in 2013, a 41.4% increase over the previous year.<sup id="cite_ref-CaribbeanInTourismGrowth_85-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CaribbeanInTourismGrowth-85">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Hato_International_Airport" class="mw-redirect" title="Hato International Airport">Hato International Airport</a> received 1,772,501 passengers in 2013 and announced capital investments totaling US$48&#160;million aimed at transforming the airport into a <a href="/wiki/Airline_hub" title="Airline hub">regional hub</a> by 2018.
</p><p>The Curaçaoan <a href="/wiki/Continental_shelf" title="Continental shelf">insular shelf's</a> sharp drop-off known as the "Blue Edge" is often visited by <a href="/wiki/Scuba_diving" title="Scuba diving">scuba diving</a> tourists.<sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> Coral reefs for <a href="/wiki/Snorkeling" title="Snorkeling">snorkeling</a> and scuba diving can be reached without a boat. The southern coast has calm waters as well as many small beaches, such as Jan Thiel and Cas Abou. At the westernmost point of the island is <a href="/wiki/Watamula" title="Watamula">Watamula</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Cliff_Villa_Peninsula" title="Cliff Villa Peninsula">Cliff Villa Peninsula</a> which are good locations for drift diving. The coastline of Curaçao features numerous bays and inlets which serve as popular mooring locations for boats.<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In June 2017, the island was named the Top Cruise Destination in the Southern Caribbean by <a href="/wiki/Cruise_Critic" title="Cruise Critic">Cruise Critic</a>, a major online forum. The winners of the Destination Awards were selected based on comments from cruise passengers who rated the downtown area of Willemstad as "amazing" and the food and shopping as "excellent".<sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> The historic centre of Willemstad is a <a href="/wiki/Lists_of_World_Heritage_Sites" title="Lists of World Heritage Sites">World Heritage Site</a>. Another attraction is the towns colourful street art. the <a href="/wiki/Blue_Bay_Sculpture_Garden" title="Blue Bay Sculpture Garden">Blue Bay Sculpture Garden</a> with works from known Curaçao artists is situated in a nearby resort.<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Landhuis_Bloemhof" title="Landhuis Bloemhof">Landhuis Bloemhof</a> is an art museum and gallery located in Willemstad.<sup id="cite_ref-beautiful_90-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-beautiful-90">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Some of the coral reefs are affected by tourism. <a href="/wiki/Playa_Porto_Marie" title="Playa Porto Marie">Porto Marie Beach</a> is experimenting with artificial coral reefs in order to improve the reef's condition.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (February 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Hundreds of artificial coral blocks that have been placed are now home to a large array of tropical fish. It is now under investigation to see if the sewer waste of hotels is a partial cause of the dying of the coral reef.<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Curacao-Sea-Aquarium-2013.JPG" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/Curacao-Sea-Aquarium-2013.JPG/220px-Curacao-Sea-Aquarium-2013.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="138" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/Curacao-Sea-Aquarium-2013.JPG/330px-Curacao-Sea-Aquarium-2013.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/Curacao-Sea-Aquarium-2013.JPG/440px-Curacao-Sea-Aquarium-2013.JPG 2x" data-file-width="4573" data-file-height="2865" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Curacao-Sea-Aquarium-2013.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The Curaçao Sea Aquarium and the Dolphin Academy share this islet on the west coast of Curaçao, with <a href="/wiki/Seaquarium_Beach" title="Seaquarium Beach">Seaquarium Beach</a> nearby.</div></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Labour">Labour</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Labour">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 2016, a Labour Force Survey (LFS) indicated that the unemployment rate was 13.3%. For residents ages 15–64, the employment rate was 70.4%.<sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Financial_services">Financial services</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Financial services">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Curaçao's history in <a href="/wiki/Financial_services" title="Financial services">financial services</a> dates back to <a href="/wiki/World_War_I" title="World War I">World War I</a>. Prior to this period, the financial arms of local merchant houses functioned as informal lenders to the community. However, at the turn of the 20th century, Curaçao underwent industrialization, and a number of merchant houses established private <a href="/wiki/Commercial_bank" title="Commercial bank">commercial banks</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup> As the economy grew, these banks began assuming additional functions eventually becoming full-fledged financial institutions.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Caribbean_Securities_Exchange" title="Dutch Caribbean Securities Exchange">Dutch Caribbean Securities Exchange</a> is located in the capital of Willemstad, as is the <a href="/wiki/Central_Bank_of_Cura%C3%A7ao_and_Sint_Maarten" title="Central Bank of Curaçao and Sint Maarten">Central Bank of Curaçao and Sint Maarten</a>; the latter of which dates to 1828. It is the oldest <a href="/wiki/Central_bank" title="Central bank">central bank</a> in the Western Hemisphere.<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup> The island's legal system supports a variety of corporate structures and is a <a href="/wiki/Corporate_haven" title="Corporate haven">corporate haven</a>. Though Curaçao is considered a <a href="/wiki/Tax_haven" title="Tax haven">tax haven</a>, it adheres to the EU Code of Conduct against harmful tax practices. It holds a qualified intermediary status from the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Internal_Revenue_Service" class="mw-redirect" title="United States Internal Revenue Service">United States Internal Revenue Service</a>. It is an accepted jurisdiction of the <a href="/wiki/OECD" title="OECD">OECD</a> and Caribbean <a href="/wiki/Financial_Action_Task_Force_on_Money_Laundering" class="mw-redirect" title="Financial Action Task Force on Money Laundering">Financial Action Task Force on Money Laundering</a>. The country enforces Anti-<a href="/wiki/Money_Laundering" class="mw-redirect" title="Money Laundering">Money Laundering</a> and <a href="/wiki/Counter-Terrorism" class="mw-redirect" title="Counter-Terrorism">Counter-Terrorism</a> funding compliance.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (September 2013)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_Account_Tax_Compliance_Act">Foreign Account Tax Compliance Act</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Foreign Account Tax Compliance Act">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Historic_Area_of_Willemstad,_Inner_City_and_Harbour,_Cura%C3%A7ao-139160.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/38/Historic_Area_of_Willemstad%2C_Inner_City_and_Harbour%2C_Cura%C3%A7ao-139160.jpg/220px-Historic_Area_of_Willemstad%2C_Inner_City_and_Harbour%2C_Cura%C3%A7ao-139160.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/38/Historic_Area_of_Willemstad%2C_Inner_City_and_Harbour%2C_Cura%C3%A7ao-139160.jpg/330px-Historic_Area_of_Willemstad%2C_Inner_City_and_Harbour%2C_Cura%C3%A7ao-139160.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/38/Historic_Area_of_Willemstad%2C_Inner_City_and_Harbour%2C_Cura%C3%A7ao-139160.jpg/440px-Historic_Area_of_Willemstad%2C_Inner_City_and_Harbour%2C_Cura%C3%A7ao-139160.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1600" data-file-height="1200" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Historic_Area_of_Willemstad,_Inner_City_and_Harbour,_Cura%C3%A7ao-139160.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Banco di Caribe in Willemstad</div></div></div>
<p>On 30 June 2014, Curaçao<sup id="cite_ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> was deemed to have an Inter-Governmental Agreement (IGA) with the United States of America with respect to the "Foreign Account Tax Compliance Act" of the United States of America. The Tax Information Exchange Agreement signed in Washington, D.C. on 17 April 2002<sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> between the U.S. and the Kingdom of the Netherlands includes Curaçao, and was updated with respect to Curaçao in 2014, taking effect in 2016.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Trade">Trade</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Trade">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Curaçao trades mainly with the United States, Venezuela, and the European Union. It has an Association Agreement with the European Union which allows companies which do business in and via Curaçao to export products to European markets,<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> free of import duties and quotas. It is also a participant in the US <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Basin_Initiative" title="Caribbean Basin Initiative">Caribbean Basin Initiative</a> allowing it to have preferential access to the US market.<sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Prostitution">Prostitution</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Prostitution">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Prostitution_in_the_Dutch_Caribbean#Curaçao" title="Prostitution in the Dutch Caribbean">Prostitution in the Dutch Caribbean §&#160;Curaçao</a></div>
<p>Prostitution in Curaçao is legal only for foreign women who get a temporary permit to work in the large open-air brothel called "Le Mirage" or "<a href="/wiki/Campo_Alegre_(brothel)" title="Campo Alegre (brothel)">Campo Alegre</a>". Using prostitution services is legal for men (locals included). The brothel has operated near the airport since the 1940s.<sup id="cite_ref-chron_100-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-chron-100">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-nswp_101-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-nswp-101">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> Curaçao monitors, contains and regulates the industry. The government states that the workers in these establishments are thereby given a safe environment and access to medical practitioners. However this approach does exclude local women (or men) to legally make a living from prostitution and does lead to loss of local income, as the foreign prostitutes send or take most of their earnings home.<sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/United_States_Department_of_State" title="United States Department of State">U.S. State Department</a> has cited anecdotal evidence claiming that, "Curaçao...[is a] destination island... for women trafficked for the sex trade from Peru, Brazil, Colombia, the Dominican Republic, and Haiti, according to local observers. At least 500 foreign women reportedly are in prostitution throughout the five islands of the Antilles, some of whom have been trafficked."<sup id="cite_ref-trafficking192_103-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-trafficking192-103">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup> The US Department of State has said that the government of Curaçao frequently underestimates the extent of <a href="/wiki/Human_trafficking" title="Human trafficking">human trafficking</a> problems.<sup id="cite_ref-trafficking192_103-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-trafficking192-103">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:302px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Santa_Famia_church_Willemstad.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/56/Santa_Famia_church_Willemstad.jpg/300px-Santa_Famia_church_Willemstad.jpg" decoding="async" width="300" height="450" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/56/Santa_Famia_church_Willemstad.jpg/450px-Santa_Famia_church_Willemstad.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/56/Santa_Famia_church_Willemstad.jpg/600px-Santa_Famia_church_Willemstad.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4235" data-file-height="6353" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Santa_Famia_church_Willemstad.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Santa Famia church, Willemstad</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Cura%C3%A7ao_synagogue1.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Cura%C3%A7ao_synagogue1.jpg/220px-Cura%C3%A7ao_synagogue1.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="293" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Cura%C3%A7ao_synagogue1.jpg/330px-Cura%C3%A7ao_synagogue1.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Cura%C3%A7ao_synagogue1.jpg/440px-Cura%C3%A7ao_synagogue1.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1944" data-file-height="2592" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Cura%C3%A7ao_synagogue1.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Mikv%C3%A9_Israel-Emanuel_Synagogue" class="mw-redirect" title="Mikvé Israel-Emanuel Synagogue">Mikvé Israel-Emanuel Synagogue</a> in Willemstad is the oldest surviving synagogue in the Western hemisphere.</div></div></div>
<p>Owing to the island's history of colonial times, the majority of the Curaçaoans are of <a href="/wiki/Afro-Cura%C3%A7aoan" class="mw-redirect" title="Afro-Curaçaoan">African</a>, or partial African descent.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao_12-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> There are also many people of <a href="/wiki/Dutch_people" title="Dutch people">Dutch</a>, French, <a href="/wiki/Latin_American" class="mw-redirect" title="Latin American">Latin American</a>, South Asian, East Asian, and <a href="/wiki/Levant" title="Levant">Levantine</a> descent.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
