<div class="thumbcaption">
<dl><dt>Religion in Curaçao<sup id="cite_ref-CIACuraçao_104-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIACuraçao-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup></dt></dl>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r981673959">.mw-parser-output .legend{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .legend-color{display:inline-block;min-width:1.25em;height:1.25em;line-height:1.25;margin:1px 0;text-align:center;border:1px solid black;background-color:transparent;color:black}.mw-parser-output .legend-text{}</style><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:DodgerBlue; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;Roman Catholic (69.8%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"/><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:SkyBlue; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;Protestant (16.7%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"/><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Black; color:white;">&#160;</span>&#160;None (10%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"/><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:grey; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;Other (Jewish, Hindu, Muslim, etc.) (3.8%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"/><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:LightGrey; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;Unspecified (0.6%)</div>
</div>
</div></div>
<p>The religious breakdown of the population of Curaçao, according to a 2011 estimate:<sup id="cite_ref-CIACuraçao_104-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIACuraçao-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholic">Roman Catholic</a>;<sup id="cite_ref-CIACuraçao_104-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIACuraçao-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> 69.8%</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Adventism" title="Adventism">Adventist</a>;<sup id="cite_ref-CIACuraçao_104-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIACuraçao-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> 9%</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Evangelicalism" title="Evangelicalism">Evangelical</a>;<sup id="cite_ref-CIACuraçao_104-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIACuraçao-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> 8.9%</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Pentecostal" class="mw-redirect" title="Pentecostal">Pentecostal</a>;<sup id="cite_ref-CIACuraçao_104-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIACuraçao-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> 7.6%</li>
<li>Other <a href="/wiki/Protestant" class="mw-redirect" title="Protestant">Protestant</a>;<sup id="cite_ref-CIACuraçao_104-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIACuraçao-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> 3.2%</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Jehovah%27s_Witnesses" title="Jehovah&#39;s Witnesses">Jehovah's Witnesses</a>;<sup id="cite_ref-CIACuraçao_104-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIACuraçao-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> 2%</li>
<li>Other;<sup id="cite_ref-CIACuraçao_104-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIACuraçao-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> 3.8%</li>
<li>None;<sup id="cite_ref-CIACuraçao_104-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIACuraçao-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> 10%</li>
<li>Unspecified;<sup id="cite_ref-CIACuraçao_104-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIACuraçao-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> 0.6%</li></ul>
<p>There has been a shift towards the Charismatic movement in recent decades. Other denominations include the <a href="/wiki/Seventh-day_Adventist_Church" title="Seventh-day Adventist Church">Seventh-day Adventist Church</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Methodist_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="Methodist Church">Methodist Church</a>. Alongside these Christian denominations, some inhabitants practise <a href="/wiki/Montamentu" title="Montamentu">Montamentu</a> and other diaspora African religions.<sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup> As elsewhere in Latin America, <a href="/wiki/Pentecostalism" title="Pentecostalism">Pentecostalism</a> is on the rise.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> There are also practising Muslims and Hindus.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Willemstad" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of Willemstad">Roman Catholic Diocese of Willemstad</a> encompasses all the territory of the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands" title="Kingdom of the Netherlands">Kingdom of the Netherlands</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean" title="Caribbean">Caribbean</a> which includes <a href="/wiki/Aruba" title="Aruba">Aruba</a>, Curaçao, <a href="/wiki/Sint_Maarten" title="Sint Maarten">Sint Maarten</a>, and the islands of <a href="/wiki/Bonaire" title="Bonaire">Bonaire</a>, <a href="/wiki/St._Eustatius" class="mw-redirect" title="St. Eustatius">St. Eustatius</a> and <a href="/wiki/Saba_(island)" title="Saba (island)">Saba</a>. The diocese is also a member of the <a href="/wiki/Antilles_Episcopal_Conference" title="Antilles Episcopal Conference">Antilles Episcopal Conference</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>While small, Curaçao's <a href="/wiki/Jewish" class="mw-redirect" title="Jewish">Jewish</a> community has had a significant impact on the island's history.<sup id="cite_ref-history_21-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-history-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> Curaçao has the oldest active Jewish congregation in the Americas, dating to 1651. The <a href="/wiki/Cura%C3%A7ao_synagogue" title="Curaçao synagogue">Curaçao synagogue</a> is the oldest synagogue of the Americas in continuous use, since its completion in 1732 on the site of a previous synagogue.<sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup> Additionally, there are both <a href="/wiki/Sephardic" class="mw-redirect" title="Sephardic">Sephardic</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ashkenazi" class="mw-redirect" title="Ashkenazi">Ashkenazi</a> Jewish communities.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao_12-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> As of the year 2000 there were approximately 300 Jewish people living on the island.<sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Languages">Languages</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Languages">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Curaçao is a <a href="/wiki/Multilingualism" title="Multilingualism">polyglot</a> society. The official languages are <a href="/wiki/Dutch_language" title="Dutch language">Dutch</a>, <a href="/wiki/Papiamentu" class="mw-redirect" title="Papiamentu">Papiamentu</a> and English.<sup id="cite_ref-official-spanish_portugues,languages_1-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-official-spanish_portugues,languages-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao_12-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> However, Dutch is the sole language for all administration and legal matters.<sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> Most of Curaçao's population is able to converse in at least two of the languages of Papiamentu, Dutch, English, and Spanish.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The most widely spoken language is Papiamentu, a <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_creole" class="mw-redirect" title="Portuguese creole">Portuguese creole</a> with African, Dutch and Spanish influences, spoken in all levels of society.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao_12-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook-_Curaçao-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> Papiamentu was introduced as a language of primary school education in 1993, making Curaçao one of a handful of places where a creole language is used as a medium to acquire basic literacy.<sup id="cite_ref-Liddicoat2007_109-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Liddicoat2007-109">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup>
Spanish and English also have a long historical presence in Curaçao. Spanish became an important language in the 18th century due to the close economic ties with Spanish territories in what are now Venezuela and Colombia<sup id="cite_ref-Putte_28-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Putte-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> and several Venezuelan TV networks are received. Use of English dates to the early 19th century, when the British took Curaçao, Aruba and <a href="/wiki/Bonaire" title="Bonaire">Bonaire</a>. When Dutch rule resumed in 1815, officials already noted wide use of the language.<sup id="cite_ref-Putte_28-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Putte-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to the 2001 census, Papiamentu is the <a href="/wiki/First_language" title="First language">first language</a> of 81.2% of the population. Dutch is the first language of 8%, Spanish of 4%, and English of 2.9%.<sup id="cite_ref-cbs_110-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cbs-110">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup> However, these numbers divide the population in terms of first language and do not account for the high rate of <a href="/wiki/Bilingualism" class="mw-redirect" title="Bilingualism">bilingualism</a> in the population of Curaçao.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Localities">Localities</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Localities">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Curaçao was divided into five districts from 1863 to 1925, after which it was reduced to the two outer districts of <a href="/wiki/Bandabou" title="Bandabou">Bandabou</a> and <a href="/wiki/Bandariba" title="Bandariba">Bandariba</a> and the city district of Willemstad. Over the years, the capital, Willemstad, encompassed the entire area surrounding the large natural harbour, the Schottegat. As a result, many formerly isolated villages have grown together to form a large urbanised area. The city covers approximately one third of the entire island in the east. Willemstad's most famous neighbourhoods are:
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Punda" class="mw-redirect" title="Punda">Punda</a>, the historic city centre with the Handelskade on St. Anna Bay.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Otrobanda" title="Otrobanda">Otrobanda</a>, on the other side of St. Anna Bay</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Pietermaai" title="Pietermaai">Pietermaai</a>, east of Punda</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Scharloo" title="Scharloo">Scharloo</a>, north of Punda and Pietermaai, across the Waaigat</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Julianadorp,_Cura%C3%A7ao" class="mw-redirect" title="Julianadorp, Curaçao">Julianadorp</a>, a suburb on the west side of the city, built around 1928 on behalf of Shell for its personnel</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Emmastad" class="mw-redirect" title="Emmastad">Emmastad</a>, built for Shell in the 1950s, after Julianadorp was full.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Sali%C3%B1a,_Cura%C3%A7ao" title="Saliña, Curaçao">Saliña</a> is situated next to Punda and has many shops and restaurants.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Brievengat" title="Brievengat">Brievengat</a>, a suburb in the north of the city.</li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Statistics">Statistics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Statistics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:552px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Cura%C3%A7ao_single_age_population_pyramid_2020.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/38/Cura%C3%A7ao_single_age_population_pyramid_2020.png/550px-Cura%C3%A7ao_single_age_population_pyramid_2020.png" decoding="async" width="550" height="442" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/38/Cura%C3%A7ao_single_age_population_pyramid_2020.png/825px-Cura%C3%A7ao_single_age_population_pyramid_2020.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/38/Cura%C3%A7ao_single_age_population_pyramid_2020.png/1100px-Cura%C3%A7ao_single_age_population_pyramid_2020.png 2x" data-file-width="2212" data-file-height="1777" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Cura%C3%A7ao_single_age_population_pyramid_2020.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Curaçao <a href="/wiki/Population_pyramid" title="Population pyramid">population pyramid</a> in 2020</div></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Hoofdingang_van_de_University_of_Curacao-Mei_2018.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/Hoofdingang_van_de_University_of_Curacao-Mei_2018.jpg/220px-Hoofdingang_van_de_University_of_Curacao-Mei_2018.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/Hoofdingang_van_de_University_of_Curacao-Mei_2018.jpg/330px-Hoofdingang_van_de_University_of_Curacao-Mei_2018.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/Hoofdingang_van_de_University_of_Curacao-Mei_2018.jpg/440px-Hoofdingang_van_de_University_of_Curacao-Mei_2018.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4608" data-file-height="3456" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Hoofdingang_van_de_University_of_Curacao-Mei_2018.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/University_of_Cura%C3%A7ao" title="University of Curaçao">University of Curaçao</a></div></div></div>
<p>Public education is based on the Dutch educational system and besides the public schools, private and parochial schools are also available. Since the introduction of a new public education law in 1992, compulsory primary education starts at age six and continues for six years; secondary lasts for another four.<sup id="cite_ref-edu_111-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-edu-111">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The main institute of higher learning is the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Cura%C3%A7ao" title="University of Curaçao">University of Curaçao</a> (formerly University of The Netherlands Antilles), enrolling 2,100 students.<sup id="cite_ref-edu_111-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-edu-111">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup> The comprehensive model of education is influenced by both the Dutch and American education systems. Other higher education offerings on the island include <a href="/wiki/Offshore_Medical_School_in_the_Caribbean" class="mw-redirect" title="Offshore Medical School in the Caribbean">offshore medical schools</a>, language schools and academies for fine art, music, police, teacher and nurse-training.<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=34" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Bulawaya-1.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/Bulawaya-1.jpg/220px-Bulawaya-1.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="194" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/Bulawaya-1.jpg/330px-Bulawaya-1.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/Bulawaya-1.jpg/440px-Bulawaya-1.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2014" data-file-height="1780" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Bulawaya-1.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A Bulawaya dance</div></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Literature">Literature</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=35" title="Edit section: Literature">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Despite the island's relatively small population, the diversity of languages and cultural influences on Curaçao have generated a remarkable literary tradition, primarily in <a href="/wiki/Dutch_language" title="Dutch language">Dutch</a> and <a href="/wiki/Papiamentu" class="mw-redirect" title="Papiamentu">Papiamentu</a>. The oral traditions of the <a href="/wiki/Arawak_peoples" class="mw-redirect" title="Arawak peoples">Arawak</a> <a href="/wiki/Indigenous_peoples" title="Indigenous peoples">indigenous peoples</a> are lost. West African slaves brought the tales of <a href="/wiki/Anansi" title="Anansi">Anansi</a>, thus forming the basis of Papiamentu literature. The first published work in Papiamentu was a poem by Joseph Sickman Corsen entitled <i>Atardi</i>, published in the <i>La Cruz</i> newspaper in 1905.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Throughout Curaçaoan literature, narrative techniques and metaphors best characterized as <a href="/wiki/Magic_realism" title="Magic realism">magic realism</a> tend to predominate. Novelists and poets from Curaçao have contributed to <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Literature" class="mw-redirect" title="Caribbean Literature">Caribbean</a> and <a href="/wiki/Dutch_literature" class="mw-redirect" title="Dutch literature">Dutch literature</a>. Best known are <a href="/wiki/Cola_Debrot" title="Cola Debrot">Cola Debrot</a>, <a href="/wiki/Frank_Martinus_Arion" title="Frank Martinus Arion">Frank Martinus Arion</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pierre_Lauffer" title="Pierre Lauffer">Pierre Lauffer</a>, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Elis_Juliana&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Elis Juliana (page does not exist)">Elis Juliana</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://nl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elis_Juliana" class="extiw" title="nl:Elis Juliana">nl</a>&#93;</span>, Guillermo Rosario, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Boeli_van_Leeuwen&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Boeli van Leeuwen (page does not exist)">Boeli van Leeuwen</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://nl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boeli_van_Leeuwen" class="extiw" title="nl:Boeli van Leeuwen">nl</a>&#93;</span> and <a href="/wiki/Tip_Marugg" title="Tip Marugg">Tip Marugg</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cuisine">Cuisine</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=36" title="Edit section: Cuisine">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Local food is called <i>Krioyo</i> (pronounced the same as <i><a href="/wiki/Criollo_people" title="Criollo people">criollo</a></i>, the Spanish word for "Creole") and boasts a blend of flavours and techniques best compared to <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_cuisine" title="Caribbean cuisine">Caribbean cuisine</a> and <a href="/wiki/Latin_American_cuisine" title="Latin American cuisine">Latin American cuisine</a>. Dishes common in Curaçao are found in <a href="/wiki/Aruba" title="Aruba">Aruba</a> and <a href="/wiki/Bonaire" title="Bonaire">Bonaire</a> as well. Popular dishes include stobá (a stew made with various ingredients such as <a href="/wiki/Papaya" title="Papaya">papaya</a>, beef or goat), Guiambo (soup made from <a href="/wiki/Okra" title="Okra">okra</a> and seafood), kadushi (cactus soup), sopi mondongo (intestine soup), funchi (cornmeal paste similar to <a href="/wiki/Fufu" title="Fufu">fufu</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ugali" title="Ugali">ugali</a> and <a href="/wiki/Polenta" title="Polenta">polenta</a>) and fish and other seafood. The ubiquitous side dish is fried <a href="/wiki/Plantain_(cooking)" class="mw-redirect" title="Plantain (cooking)">plantain</a>. Local bread rolls are made according to a Portuguese recipe. All around the island, there are snèks which serve local dishes as well as alcoholic drinks in a manner akin to the English <a href="/wiki/Public_house" class="mw-redirect" title="Public house">public house</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The ubiquitous breakfast dish is pastechi: fried pastry with fillings of cheese, tuna, ham, or ground meat. Around the holiday season special dishes are consumed, such as the <a href="/wiki/Hallaca" title="Hallaca">hallaca</a> and pekelé, made out of <a href="/wiki/Salt_cod" class="mw-redirect" title="Salt cod">salt cod</a>. At weddings and other special occasions a variety of kos dushi are served: kokada (coconut sweets), ko'i lechi (condensed milk and sugar sweet) and tentalaria (peanut sweets). The <a href="/wiki/Cura%C3%A7ao_liqueur" class="mw-redirect" title="Curaçao liqueur">Curaçao liqueur</a> was developed here, when a local experimented with the rinds of the local <a href="/wiki/Citrus" title="Citrus">citrus</a> fruit known as <a href="/wiki/Laraha" title="Laraha">laraha</a>. <a href="/wiki/Suriname" title="Suriname">Surinamese</a>, Chinese, Indonesian, Indian and Dutch culinary influences also abound. The island also has a number of Chinese restaurants that serve mainly Indonesian dishes such as <a href="/wiki/Satay" title="Satay">satay</a>, <a href="/wiki/Nasi_goreng" title="Nasi goreng">nasi goreng</a> and <a href="/wiki/Lumpia" title="Lumpia">lumpia</a> (which are all Indonesian names for the dishes). Dutch specialties such as <a href="/wiki/Croquette" title="Croquette">croquettes</a> and <a href="/wiki/Oliebollen" class="mw-redirect" title="Oliebollen">oliebollen</a> are widely served in homes and restaurants.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sports">Sports</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=37" title="Edit section: Sports">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Jurickson_Profar_(47569154041).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/Jurickson_Profar_%2847569154041%29.jpg/220px-Jurickson_Profar_%2847569154041%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="158" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/Jurickson_Profar_%2847569154041%29.jpg/330px-Jurickson_Profar_%2847569154041%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/Jurickson_Profar_%2847569154041%29.jpg/440px-Jurickson_Profar_%2847569154041%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5565" data-file-height="4000" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Jurickson_Profar_(47569154041).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Jurickson_Profar" title="Jurickson Profar">Jurickson Profar</a></div></div></div>
<p>In 2004, the <a href="/wiki/Little_League_Baseball" title="Little League Baseball">Little League Baseball</a> team from Willemstad, Curaçao, won the world title in a game against the United States champion from <a href="/wiki/Thousand_Oaks,_California" title="Thousand Oaks, California">Thousand Oaks, California</a>. The Willemstad lineup included <a href="/wiki/Jurickson_Profar" title="Jurickson Profar">Jurickson Profar</a>, the standout shortstop prospect who now plays for the <a href="/wiki/San_Diego_Padres" title="San Diego Padres">San Diego Padres</a> of <a href="/wiki/Major_League_Baseball" title="Major League Baseball">Major League Baseball</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Jonathan_Schoop" title="Jonathan Schoop">Jonathan Schoop</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Curaçaoan players <a href="/wiki/Andruw_Jones" title="Andruw Jones">Andruw Jones</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Ozzie_Albies" title="Ozzie Albies">Ozzie Albies</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Kenley_Jansen" title="Kenley Jansen">Kenley Jansen</a> have made multiple <a href="/wiki/Major_League_Baseball_All-Star_Game" title="Major League Baseball All-Star Game">Major League Baseball All-Star Game</a> appearances.<sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The 2010 documentary film <i>Boys of Summer</i><sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> details Curaçao's Pabao Little League All-Stars winning their country's eighth straight championship at the 2008 <a href="/wiki/Little_League_World_Series" title="Little League World Series">Little League World Series</a>, then going on to defeat other teams, including Puerto Rico and the Dominican Republic, and earning a spot in <a href="/wiki/South_Williamsport,_Pennsylvania" title="South Williamsport, Pennsylvania">Williamsport</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The prevailing <a href="/wiki/Trade_wind" class="mw-redirect" title="Trade wind">trade winds</a> and warm water make Curaçao a location for <a href="/wiki/Windsurfing" title="Windsurfing">windsurfing</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There is warm, clear water around the island. <a href="/wiki/Scuba_dive" class="mw-redirect" title="Scuba dive">Scuba divers</a> and snorkelers may have visibility up to 30 metres (98 feet) at the Curaçao Underwater Marine Park, which stretches along 20 kilometres (12 miles) of Curaçao's southern coastline.<sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Curaçao participated in the <a href="/wiki/2013_CARIFTA_Games" title="2013 CARIFTA Games">2013 CARIFTA Games</a>. Kevin Philbert stood third in the under-20 male Long Jump with a distance of 7.36 metres (24.15 feet). Vanessa Philbert stood second the under-17 female 1,500 metres (4,900 feet) with a time of 4:47.97.<sup id="cite_ref-cfpi_1_121-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cfpi_1-121">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-milesplit_1_122-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-milesplit_1-122">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-cfpi_2_123-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cfpi_2-123">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-milesplit_2_124-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-milesplit_2-124">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Cura%C3%A7ao_national_football_team" title="Curaçao national football team">Curaçao national football team</a> won the <a href="/wiki/2017_Caribbean_Cup" title="2017 Caribbean Cup">2017 Caribbean Cup</a> by defeating <a href="/wiki/Jamaica_national_football_team" title="Jamaica national football team">Jamaica</a> in the final, qualifying for the <a href="/wiki/2017_CONCACAF_Gold_Cup" title="2017 CONCACAF Gold Cup">2017 CONCACAF Gold Cup</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> They then traveled to <a href="/wiki/Thailand" title="Thailand">Thailand</a> and participated in the <a href="/wiki/2019_King%27s_Cup" title="2019 King&#39;s Cup">2019 King's Cup</a> for the first time, eventually winning the tournament by beating <a href="/wiki/Vietnam" title="Vietnam">Vietnam</a> in the final.<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Infrastructure">Infrastructure</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=38" title="Edit section: Infrastructure">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Airport">Airport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=39" title="Edit section: Airport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p><a href="/wiki/Cura%C3%A7ao_International_Airport" title="Curaçao International Airport">Curaçao International Airport</a> (also called Hato International Airport) is located on the northern coast of the island and offers connections to the Caribbean region, South America, North America and Europe. Curaçao Airport is a fairly large facility, with the third longest commercial runway in the Caribbean region after Rafael Hernández Airport in Puerto Rico and <a href="/wiki/Pointe-%C3%A0-Pitre_International_Airport" title="Pointe-à-Pitre International Airport">Pointe-à-Pitre International Airport</a> in Guadeloupe. The airport served as a main base for <a href="/wiki/Insel_Air" title="Insel Air">Insel Air</a>, and for <a href="/wiki/Air_ALM" class="mw-redirect" title="Air ALM">Air ALM</a>, the former national airlines of Curaçao.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Railways">Railways</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=40" title="Edit section: Railways">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 1887 a horse drawn street tramway opened in Punda, the part of the capital Willemstad on the eastern side of Sint Annabaai. It had a U-shaped route about 2 km in length. In 1896, a tramway opened in Otrabanda on the opposite side of the bay, but it ceased operations within a few months. The Punda line was rebuilt in 1911, regauged to metre gauge, and the horse drawn trams replaced by petrol engined ones. The line closed in 1920.<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Bridges">Bridges</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=41" title="Edit section: Bridges">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Curacao-Bridges-2013.JPG" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/Curacao-Bridges-2013.JPG/220px-Curacao-Bridges-2013.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="149" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/Curacao-Bridges-2013.JPG/330px-Curacao-Bridges-2013.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/Curacao-Bridges-2013.JPG/440px-Curacao-Bridges-2013.JPG 2x" data-file-width="4608" data-file-height="3130" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Curacao-Bridges-2013.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Queen_Emma_Bridge" title="Queen Emma Bridge">Queen Emma</a> (semi-open), and the <a href="/wiki/Queen_Juliana_Bridge" title="Queen Juliana Bridge">Queen Juliana</a></div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Smith_brug_over_the_Waaigat.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dd/Smith_brug_over_the_Waaigat.jpg/220px-Smith_brug_over_the_Waaigat.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dd/Smith_brug_over_the_Waaigat.jpg/330px-Smith_brug_over_the_Waaigat.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dd/Smith_brug_over_the_Waaigat.jpg/440px-Smith_brug_over_the_Waaigat.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4032" data-file-height="3024" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Smith_brug_over_the_Waaigat.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Smith brug over the Waaigat</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Kon._Wilhelmina_brug_over_the_Waaigat.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/Kon._Wilhelmina_brug_over_the_Waaigat.jpg/220px-Kon._Wilhelmina_brug_over_the_Waaigat.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/Kon._Wilhelmina_brug_over_the_Waaigat.jpg/330px-Kon._Wilhelmina_brug_over_the_Waaigat.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/Kon._Wilhelmina_brug_over_the_Waaigat.jpg/440px-Kon._Wilhelmina_brug_over_the_Waaigat.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4032" data-file-height="3024" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Kon._Wilhelmina_brug_over_the_Waaigat.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Kon. Wilhelmina brug over the Waaigat</div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Queen_Emma_Bridge" title="Queen Emma Bridge">Queen Emma Bridge</a>, a 168 metres (551&#160;ft) long <a href="/wiki/Pontoon_bridge" title="Pontoon bridge">pontoon bridge</a>, connects pedestrians between the Punda and Otrobanda districts.<sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> This swings open to allow the passage of ships to and from the port.<sup id="cite_ref-queen_129-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-queen-129">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup> The bridge was originally opened in 1888 and the current bridge was installed in 1939.<sup id="cite_ref-emmabridge_130-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-emmabridge-130">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> It is best known and, more often than not, referred to by the locals as "Our Swinging Old Lady".<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Queen_Juliana_Bridge" title="Queen Juliana Bridge">Queen Juliana Bridge</a> connects mobile traffic between the same two districts. At 185 feet (56&#160;m) above the sea, it is one of the highest bridges in the Caribbean.<sup id="cite_ref-queen_129-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-queen-129">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Utilities_and_sanitation">Utilities and sanitation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=42" title="Edit section: Utilities and sanitation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Aqualectra, a government-owned company<sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup> and full member of <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Electric_Utility_Services_Corporation_(CARILEC)" class="mw-redirect" title="Caribbean Electric Utility Services Corporation (CARILEC)">CARILEC</a>, delivers potable water and electricity to the island. Rates are controlled by the government. Water is produced by <a href="/wiki/Reverse_osmosis" title="Reverse osmosis">reverse osmosis</a> or <a href="/wiki/Desalinization" class="mw-redirect" title="Desalinization">desalinization</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-aqualectra.com_133-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-aqualectra.com-133">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup> It services 69,000 households and companies using 130,000 water and electric meters.<sup id="cite_ref-aqualectra.com_133-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-aqualectra.com-133">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup> The power generation company NuCuraçao opened wind farms in Tera Kora and Playa Kanoa in 2012, and expanded in Tera Kora in 2015.<sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> There is no natural gas distribution grid; gas is supplied to homes by pressurized containers.<sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Kerbside_collection" title="Kerbside collection">Curbside trash pickup</a> is provided by the Selikor company. There is no <a href="/wiki/Recycling" title="Recycling">recycling</a> pickup, but there are drop-off centers for certain recycled materials at the Malpais <a href="/wiki/Landfill" title="Landfill">landfill</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup> and various locations operated by Green Force;<sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-138" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-138">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup> private haulers recycle <a href="/wiki/Construction_waste" title="Construction waste">construction waste</a>, paper, and cardboard.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-140" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-140">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notable_residents">Notable residents</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=43" title="Edit section: Notable residents">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>People from Curaçao include:
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Arts_and_culture">Arts and culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=44" title="Edit section: Arts and culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Izaline_Calister" title="Izaline Calister">Izaline Calister</a>, singer-songwriter</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Joceline_Clemencia" title="Joceline Clemencia">Joceline Clemencia</a>, writer</li>

<li><a href="/wiki/Tip_Marugg" title="Tip Marugg">Tip Marugg</a>, writer<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2014)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup></li>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Politics_and_government">Politics and government</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=45" title="Edit section: Politics and government">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>

<li><a href="/wiki/George_Maduro" title="George Maduro">George Maduro</a>, a war hero and namesake of <a href="/wiki/Madurodam" title="Madurodam">Madurodam</a> in <a href="/wiki/The_Hague" title="The Hague">The Hague</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Manuel_Piar" title="Manuel Piar">Manuel Carlos Piar</a>, general and competitor of Bolivar during the <a href="/wiki/Venezuelan_War_of_Independence" title="Venezuelan War of Independence">Venezuelan War of Independence</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Tula_(Cura%C3%A7ao)" title="Tula (Curaçao)">Tula</a>, leader of the 1795 <a href="/wiki/Slave_rebellion" title="Slave rebellion">slave revolt</a></li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sports_2">Sports</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=46" title="Edit section: Sports">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Baseball">Baseball</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cura%C3%A7ao&amp;action=edit&amp;section=47" title="Edit section: Baseball">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Players in Minor League Baseball:<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (October 2017)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Sharlon_Schoop" title="Sharlon Schoop">Sharlon Schoop</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> professional infielder</li></ul>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Netherlands_Antilles_national_baseball_team" title="Netherlands Antilles national baseball team">Netherlands Antilles national baseball team</a></div>
<p>Players in <a href="/wiki/Major_League_Baseball" title="Major League Baseball">Major League Baseball</a>:
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Ozzie_Albies" title="Ozzie Albies">Ozzie Albies</a>, professional second baseman<sup id="cite_ref-147" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-147">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Wladimir_Balentien" title="Wladimir Balentien">Wladimir Balentien</a>, professional <a href="/wiki/Outfielder" title="Outfielder">outfielder</a><sup id="cite_ref-148" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-148">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup></li>

</div></td></tr><tr><td class="navbox-abovebelow" colspan="2"><div>
<ul><li><sup><small>3</small></sup> <span class="wrap">Became <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands#Countries" title="Kingdom of the Netherlands">constituent countries</a> of the Kingdom of the Netherlands; <a href="/wiki/Suriname_(Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands)" title="Suriname (Kingdom of the Netherlands)">Suriname</a> gained <a href="/wiki/Suriname" title="Suriname">full independence</a> in 1975, Curaçao and Dependencies was renamed to the <a href="/wiki/Netherlands_Antilles" title="Netherlands Antilles">Netherlands Antilles</a>, which was eventually dissolved in 2010.</span></li></ul>
</div></td></tr></tbody></table><div></div></td></tr></tbody></table><div></div></td></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="navbox-list navbox-odd" style="width:100%;padding:0"><div style="padding:0 0.25em"></div><table class="nowraplinks mw-collapsible uncollapsed navbox-subgroup" style="border-spacing:0"><tbody><tr><th scope="col" class="navbox-title" colspan="2"><div id="Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands_(1954–present)" style="font-size:114%;margin:0 4em"><a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands" title="Kingdom of the Netherlands">Kingdom of the Netherlands</a> (1954–present)</div></th></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="navbox-list navbox-odd" style="width:100%;padding:0"><div style="padding:0 0.25em"></div><table class="nowraplinks navbox-subgroup" style="border-spacing:0"><tbody><tr><th scope="row" class="navbox-group" style="width:1%">Constituent countries</th><td class="navbox-list-with-group navbox-list navbox-odd hlist" style="width:100%;padding:0"><div style="padding:0 0.25em">
